2.2 Reservoir Information

2.2.1 Key Reservoir Descriptions

Fahliyan Formation: This carbonate to argillaceous carbonate Formation is limited by overlying thick argillaceous successions in the upper part (eaquivalent to the Iraqi Ratawi Formation) and the main part consists of different bioclastic limestones with some layers of shale and argillaceous facies that can be assumed equivalent with Iraqi Yamama Formation. However in South Azadegan wells-1, 2 and 5 the Fahliyan Formation is characterized by fossiliferous limestone beds and interbedded shales. It should be noted that most of Majnoon wells penetrated into the Sarvak and Fahliyan carbonates as one of the main reservoir targets.

2.3.1 Expected Stratigraphy and Formation Top in F10:

The area is completely covered by the alluvium of Holocene age; the drilled will be Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, Gadvan and Fahliyan formations. A brief stratigraphic sequence has been predicted as follows :

- Aghajari Formation (surface to 1085.3mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 1075.7 m)

The Aghajari Formation consists of a repetition of brown to grey calcareous sandstones, chert debris and gypsumveined red marl. A fauna of brackish to freshwater was observed, together with more marine fauna.. Aghajari overlays Gachsaran Formation with a conformable boundary.

- Gachsaran Formation (1085.3-1439.3 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 354m)

The Gachsaran Formation with a thickness ranges from 400 m to 500 m thickens towards the north. Deposition of the Gachsaran Formation is cyclic. The cycle began with a deposition of shallow-water marine carbonates, and then it was overlain by a thick evaporitic phase. The evaporates were capped by red to variegated marl, which represented the end of cycle. Gachsaran is laid on Asmari Formation with a comformable boundary.

- Asmari Formation (1439.3-1712.9 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 273.6 m)

The Asmari Formation in Azadegan Field developed a massive sandstone interval, with a few thin argillaceous limestones in the upper sections. Sandstone are clear, fine to medium-sorted, and poorly consolidated. On the other hand, the lower sections consist of intercalation of sandy, chalky, slightly dolomitic high-energy limestone with sandstone layers. Asmari is parallel to Pabdeh Formation at the bottom.

- Pabdeh Formation (1712.5-2204.8 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 491.9 m)

Pabdeh Formation is divided into two parts in the Azadegan field. The upper part is made of grey to dark-brown argillaceous limestones and marls. Planktonic fauna dates that this part was deposited in Oligocene. The lower part is composed of dark-brown, slightly argillaceous limestone. Pelagic fauna, mostly Globigerina and Globorotalia, indicates that this part was formed during late Paleocene to late Eocene in age. It seems that no early Paleocene Formation exists in the Azadegan area. Chert and cherty limestone layers at the bottom of Pabdeh Formation are very important for drilling parameters on the PDC bit (as a notification for drilling operation department).

- Gurpi Formation (2204.8-2558.9mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 354.1 m)

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 20

Gurpi Formation consists of argillaceous limestone with higher clay content rather than Pabdeh Formation. About 50 meters of pure limestone will be observed in the middle part of the formation which divides it into 3 different parts as below:

- Upper Gurpi (2204.8-2351.6mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 146.8 m)

The Gurpi Formation in the Azadegan Field consists of grey to dark-brownish, slightly argillaceous micritic limestone with Maestrichtian to Campanian aged fauna. At Well AZN-01, about 50 meters of chalky limestones were encountered in the middle of the Gurpi formation, which contains abundant fragments of rudists, bryozoa, corals, and Orbitoides.

- Tarbur Member (2351.6-2424.1 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 72.5 m)

In the western Abadan Plain about 50 meters thick chalky limestone was encountered in the middle part of the Gurpi Formation, which contains abundant fragments of bioclasts, suggesting very shallow depositional environment. This interval is equivalnt to the Tarbur Formation with reservoir quality in other regions.

- Lower Gurpi (2424.1-2558.9 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 134.8 m)

This member consists of gray, light gray, medium hard argillaceous Limestone. The Gurpi formation is overlain directly on Ilam formation by conformity contact.

- Ilam Formation (2558.9-2640.5 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 81.6 m)

In the Azadegan Field, the Ilam Formation consists of grey micritic limestone, often argillaceous, rarely pyritic, limestones. Microfauna, mostly pelagic, including rotalids and oligostegina assemblage, a few algae, bryozoa, and rudist fragments exist.

- Laffan Member (2640.5-2647.2mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 6.7 m)

The Laffan Formation developed a thin greenish-grey pyritic shale layer with ostracods fauna that capping the Sarvak Formation.

- Sarvak Formation (2647.2-3292.7 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 645.5 m)

The Sarvak Formation developed a carbonate unit, which was gradually overlying the Kazhdumi Formation, and it is abruptly terminated by an erosion phase, covered by a various late Cretaceous sediment. The lithology of Sarvak consists of the dark grey fine grained nodular argillaceous limestone interbedded with thin marls in the bottom. The Limestone graded upward into a white-to-buff massive chalky limestone with siliceous nodules; developed a very massive tan-to-brown limestone containing abundant rudist debris in the middle part. In the upper part is made up

of rubbly limestone with red staining and leached zones of breccia and the top of Sarvak Formation is very ferruginous.

- Kazhdumi Formation (3292.7-3488 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 195.3 m)

Kazhdumi Formation begins with dark brown, soft to medium hard, some fissile and calcareous Shale. This formation is start with dark brown to dark green gray, medium hard to hard, soft to medium, high bituminous, calcareous Shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone, sandy claystone, claystone, marl, sandy limestone, limestone beds. The Argillaceous Limestone is dark brown to brown gray, medium hard to hard, micro bituminous. The Sandy claystone is light green gray, soft, high calcareous. The Claystone is gray to light gray, soft. The Marl is green gray, soft, sticky. The Sandy Limestone is dark green gray, hard, faint sandy. The Limestone is light gray to green gray, medium hard to soft, partly argillaceous. The Kazhdumi Formation at base is consisting of Azadegan Member.

- Dariyan Formation (3488-3649.2 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 161.2 m)

In Dariyan Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones were deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan formation divided to three part:Dariyan formation starts with white to white cream grey clean limestone type I/II, I. Base of Dariyan formation, appears grey, green Pyritic shale interbedded with argillaceous limestone. This zone called transition zone and thickness is 16m. after last grey limestone indicates of Gadvan formation.

This carbonate formation contains three zones. D1 and D3 are non-reservoirs and D2 is a moderately poor reservoir.

- Gadvan Formation (3649.2-3942.6 mMD) (Drilled Thickness: 293.4 m)

In This Formation a variety of lithological rock types such as carbonates, rich organic shales and sandstones were deposited in various environments. The lower part of the Gadvan Formation is composed of low-energy, but not very deep, micritic, often argillaceous limestone with echinoids and algal fragments. Two sandstone units were defined in the Gadvan Formation lateral changes and especially discontinuity of the sandstone layers which are consist of fine clastics (shales and siltstones) may be expected

The Gadvan Formation divided to three part;

Upper Gadvan; the upper part starts with occurrence of a terrigenous rock. The lithology is composed of grey claystone and some shale.

The Argillaceous Limestone is gray white gray medium hard to soft, partly pyritic. The Limestone is white, white to light gray, hard. The Claystone is light gray, soft, sticky. 2.3.4 Mud Logging Activities in F10

Mud logging unit will be spudded at the start of the drilling project containing all standard sensors for controlling the activities. In geological operation, the required mud logging activites are as below:

- Depth controlling

- Lag depth calculation through mud Pump GPM and drilling depth

- Gas samples gathering, analysis and giving the related reports

- Oil show samples gathering and including the results in daily reports.

- Colaboration with directional drilling section in order for performing related calculations

- Catching the cutting samples, describe the samples and providing graphic logs

- Providing daily, weekly and monthly reports according to the company formats

- Preparing the Well Geological Final Report according to the standard formats.

2.4 General Assumptions

2.4.1 Well Objective, Target and Total Depth

Purpose of the Well (Objectives):

This well will be completed as a fahliyan oil producer at vertical hole.

Wellbore Quality Evaluation Criteria:

1) Maximum Deviation and Horizontal Displacement for Vertical Wells

Depth(m) Deviation (°)Bottom Hole Shift (m)DLS（°/30m）≤2000 ≤3° ≤50 0≤2.25°2000~3000 ≤5° ≤80 ≤2.50°3000~4000 ≤7° ≤120 ≤2.75°4000~5000 ≤9° ≤160 ≤3°

2) Other requirements for Wellbore Quality

⚫ Average borehole diameter enlargement rate less than 15%

⚫ All casing/liner are run to designed depth (normally 1-2m above TD/section TD)

⚫ No fills at bottom of hole

⚫ No junk lost in hole

⚫ No abnormal casing wear and casing deformation caused by CONTRACTOR’s malpractice

3) Well trajectory control key points

• Drill upper formation before KOP using Pendulum BHA, low WOB and high RPM, to ensure vertical hole

drilled.

• Control the distance between two holes in one well pad is not less than 2 meters. Single-shot and multi-shot

will be used to monitor the well trajectory. Take single shot survey at interval of 100m; run multi-shot survey

each 300m from surface to kick off point. If necessary, survey shall be run more frequently.

• Run build up BHA (Motor + MWD) whenever collision risk increases to build up a little inclination (2~4°) and

bottom hole departure to avoid collision at vertical section if necessary.

• If magnetic interference exists, gyroscopic inclinometer shall be used in order to avoid collision.

• Amend the planned profile according to the data obtained by electronic multi-shot or single-shot survey in

vertical hole, and locate the kick off point.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 40

• MWD/GR will be used in directional drilling to ensure that the actual well path will coincide with the planned

well trajectory to the extent that it shall not pass through the boundaries of the corridor that surrounds the

planned hole trajectory in case a corridor has been specified.

• LWD/GR will be utilized in horizontal drilling in order to controlling well trajectory, especially in Sarvak-3.

• The downhole motor and MWD shall be used in vertical hole section if necessary.

• Monitors and analyses the build-up ability of the BHA used in directional/horizontal section. Due to the

lagged survey data measured by MWD, inclination, azimuth and the buildup rate should be predicted based

on the instant survey at all times. If necessary, more surveys should be taken so as to adjust drilling

parameters and BHA, control the well trajectory within the specified target tolerance strictly.

• In buildup section, to avoid pipe-stuck, BHA should be designing such way to minimize sliding mode time

especially in clay formations. To reduce torque in rotating mode and consequently reduce sliding mode time,

using stabilizer with short gauge and profile.

• Using circulating sub above MWD is recommended due to sever loss in this area.

• Adjust the drilling method according to the well survey data, and control the change amplitude of inclination

and azimuth to ensure the smoothness of the well trajectory.

• In this section, the torque and drag are relatively higher, torque and drag analysis for each BHA run should

be performed, and torque and drag reduction measures (for example simplifying the BHA or running cuttings

bed impeller) are recommended.

2.4.2 Expected H2S and CO2

Other risks are taken into consideration in this oilfield such as corrosion gas H2S, CO2 as following.

The combination of H2S and CO2 is more aggressive than H2S alone, since it possibly causes drill string and casing corrosion and drilling crew harmed or poisoned

2.4.3 Depth Refrence

All depths are based on RTE=13.45m

4.Sequence of Drilling Operation

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 60

4.1 Rig Move

➢ Hold pre-move meeting on location. Discuss contingency plans for:

• Safe arrangements for rig up and rig down.

• Safe standby areas for rig

• In case move have to be suspended.

• Loss of trucks in case of repair.

• Moving at night.

➢ Hold pre-move safety meeting. Ensure everyone understands individual responsibilities:

➢ Commence rigging down. The ordering of trucks will be done by rig manager.

➢ Commence rig move to F10, PAD NO. 64

• Position rig for optimal operations

➢ Rig up on cellar

• FinIIF rig up and commence operational procedure for (F10)

• Before leaving previous location, all equipment should be checked.

➢ Test all manifold and surface safety valves

• RKB – GLE and Elevation

• Spud date and time.

➢ Mud tanks should be empty during move. Fill water tanks and keep as full as possible.

4.2 Pre spud

Certificate

➢ All rig equipment is on location, properly rigged up and function-tested. Check availability and

content of hoisting equipment inspection &amp;amp; maintenance records according to API.

Safety Device

➢ Test draw-works crown block saver and record results on daily drilling report; test derrick man escape

device (Geronimo line) and record time required for test weight on daily drilling report; test rig intercom

system, especially for Driller-Derrick Man and Driller-Mud Logger communication link.

➢ Check that all rig safety equipment, e.g., fire extinguIIFers, eye-wash stations etc., is installed at the proper

locations as per rig layout diagrams.

➢ Verify that substructure is level and foundation adequate to support drilling unit during whole well period

and that mast is centred above rotary table.

➢ Ensure rig alignment with Top Drive System. Make sure rig tongs and rig floor hoisting equipment are

adequate for handling 8-1/2″ and 9-1/2″ drill collars. Make sure that mechanical tong torque gauges for drill

pipe/drill collar are properly functioning and have been calibrated; re-calibrated as required.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 61

Rig Equipment

➢ Verify that the 26″ tri-cone bit is ready to be picked up, all nozzles installed, and 26″ bit breakers are on the rig

floor. Make sure single-shot or multi-shot survey tools available for vertical section survey.

➢ Make sure all solids control equipment are correctly installed and have been function-tested with charging

pressure gauges in place where applicable. Make sure adequate screen inventory for the shale shakers is

available in the sizes required for the well. Make sure water tanks are completely filled and transfer lines from

water tanks to mud system are hooked up. Make sure all mud materials and chemicals required to drill 26″ section

is on location, in sound condition and suitably stored. Make sure that bentonite is pre-hydrated and sufficient

volume has been built for top section and for well-kill.

➢ Make sure that all mud pumps have been fitted with new consumable parts: liners, pistons, valves, seats etc.

Make sure that pulsation dampeners are charged with Nitrogen to the proper pre-charge pressure, 30% of

expected standpipe pressure during drilling of the 26″ hole section; check if sufficient Nitrogen bottles are available

on location to adjust pulsation dampener pre-charge pressure as required. Make sure that mud pump pop-off

valves are set at the proper pressure for the liner sizes installed and that the mud discharge lines from the popoff valves to the mud tanks are properly secured. Check that all high-pressure hoses are properly secured with

safety chains. P/T should be done to high-pressure surface line of circulating system.

Wellhead &amp;amp; BOP

➢ Have 2″ line pipe nipple with 2″ ball valve welded to 30″ conductor pipe 0.2m above cellar bottom to drain

cement from conductor/riser pipe after cementing 20″ surface casing. Verify that pre-installed 30″ conductor

pipe is vertical and that no angle/bend exists between conductor and 30″ riser pipe.

➢ Make sure that adequate supply of 20″ casing is on location, inspected and ready to be run, float shoe

should already be made up, centralizers should be pre-installed on the pipe-rack prior to spud in the well.

➢ Ensure mud logging unit is functioning and equipment are calibrated and tested properly.

Others

➢ Verify that the rig’s fuel tanks are full to meet minimum of three days rig consumption available on site.

➢ Make sure all waste treatment equipment available and commissioning test is ok.

➢ Hold pre-spud meeting with relevant service Company personnel on location. Discuss procedures involved

throughout 26″ hole section drilling, reporting system and critical safety operation measures.

➢ NIDC prepared a pre-spud checklist and distributed it in rigs. The checklist will be performed based on the

aforementioned checklist. In addition to the checklist, following matters should be checked:

➢ Check &amp;amp; observe the alignment of the rig. This check will be performed in four stages; rig levelling, derrick

verticality, derrick alignment with rotary table, rotary table alignment with conductor pipe. To do this pick up 3

joints of 9 3/4" DC and check the verticality of the derrick.

➢ Mechanical facilities for mud treatment such as:

1. Desander, Desilter, mud cleaner, proper size of shaker–screens, hoppers, mud guns &amp;amp; mixers, should be all available on rig and functioning properly.

2. All mud tanks, mud transfer lines &amp;amp; connection should be checked by filling them with water &amp;amp; cross wellhead circulation.

3. Mud engineer laboratory should be equipped with a complete set of testing equipment. The calibration should be done under supervision of well site drilling supervisor before starting the operation.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 62

4. Weight indicator, rotary speed gauge, any smart drilling instrumentation, Top Drive, Stroke meter, pressure gauges on manifold and in the drilling console, loss / gain gauge and any other gauges should be checked and calibrated.

5. Stand pipe manifold valves &amp;amp; connection must be checked &amp;amp; pressure tested.

6. All motors should be checked under load prior to start drilling

7. Check &amp;amp; control mud stock &amp;amp; cement stock every day &amp;amp; if there is any necessity to request mud material, cement or any drilling tool, it should be prepared &amp;amp; requested at proper time.

Note: Fill up following forms and send to drilling office:

➢ Pre-spud check list

➢ Location Handed Over

4.3Drilling

4.3.1 26” Hole Section, 20” Casing

General

After commencing spud-in, 26” hole drilling will be started. The drilling would be anticipated to go on fast.

Casing Point 20” Casing @ 150mFormation AghajariRisks &amp;amp; Hole problems Bit balled upDeviation control INCLINOMETER (TOTCO)Logging ---Bit Type 1-1-1 (1-1-5)Drilling FluidPre-Hydrated Bentonite mudMW 65-67PV 5-18Vis. &amp;gt;50Drilling ParametersWOB 10-30GPM 900-1000RPM Max available

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 63

Sequence of Operation

• Make up 26” BHA as per BHA design. Tag formation at ±14m.

• Drill ahead formation down to 151m (20” casing point in Aghajari formation) using Hi-vis bentonite pill. Pay

enough attention to drill straight hole.

Note:

In Case of complete loss while drillin 26” hole Cure by pump LCM pill or cement as cement plug.

➢ TD should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole below casing shoe.

➢ The exact TD will be determined based on casing tally.

➢ Run Inclinometer (Totco) each 50m to evaluate the verticality of the hole.

➢ Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site and are in serviceable condition.

➢ Ensure that adequate fIIFing tools for this section of hole are available.

➢ Enough Mud Material, LCM and cement should be available on rig site to use in case of needing.

➢ At TD, Circulate and clean the hole. Spot high viscosity pill at bottom, drop Inclinometer (Totco) POOH to

surface. Check Bit.

➢ Perform hole condition prior to run casing. If observe any indication of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and

ream to make sure that the hole condition is good.

➢ Perform safety meeting.

➢ R/U casing running equipment.

➢ Run ±150m (Approx. 13 Jts) 20", 94 ppf, K-55, BTC casing with stab-in duplex shoe to bottom as per casing

program. Casing shoe should be adjusted to have 1m of rat hole and enough stick up (1 m on rig floor). Fill

casing by non-LCM fresh mud.

Note:

➢ Take care when running casing not to set down excessive weight if tight hole or ledges are encountered.

➢ Run stinger on DP’s using the “C” Plate &amp;amp; 5” x 20” centralizer. Sting in. M/U &amp;amp; test cementing lines. Circulate

for one casing annulus volume. While circulating, check the DPs annulus for any return. Use all DPs weight

on the string if observing any minor leakage through DPs annulus.

➢ Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done in due time.

Displace with mud (1-2 bbls Short Displace). Monitor for pressures. Record and report the final pressure,

Sting out. Check the float. POOH one stand, Circulate to flush/clean drill pipe.

➢ After cement returns to cellar floor, open the 2" nipple on conductor &amp;amp; flush it.

➢ POOH to surface. L/D stinger.

➢ Keep the casing on the elevator until cement surface samples get hard. Check for casing to be center inside

conductor pipe.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 64

Note:

➢ Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top jobs.

➢ If there is no cement return to surface, a top job should be performed to fill the annulus. Use same slurry

weight as the primary cement slurry. Ensure the availability of adequate X-over for connecting line pipe to

cement line. The cement should be accelerated with max. (2%) Calcium Chloride BWOC and no other

additives. Wait for the top job cement to get hard.

➢ Check casing by slack off weight.

➢ Cut conductor pipe. Rough cut landing joint and L/D C.P. and L.J. Dress and bevel 20” suitable to install and

to weld casing head housing.

➢ Install and weld Casing Head Housing 20 ¾” – 3000 psi on casing and pressure test the sealing of welded

casing. (Limit pressure to a maximum of 80% of the collapse strength of the casing, recommended

testing pressure: 400 psi). Outlets must be aligning with cellar stairs.

➢ Before installing the casing head housing, open all the test ports and clean inside it with water and air.

➢ Check the 20 ¾” wear bushing, 20 ¾” Plug Tester and 13-3/8” Casing Slip and seal assembly in CHH before

installing CHH (on Surface).

➢ N/U 21 ¼”-3000 BOP stack and related lines and valves and test all to 2400psi.

➢ N/U Bell Nipple &amp;amp; flow line

➢ P/U &amp;amp; M/U Next BHA and stand drill pipes to 2211m.

➢ Fill up the following forms and send to drilling office:

Casing Tally

BHA reports

Casing Report

Cement Report

Wellhead diagram

Hole Section Summary for EOWR

4.3.2 17-1/2” Hole Section, 13-3/8” Casing

General

Casing Point 13-3/8” Casing @ ±2210m (5m TVDbelow top of Gurpi FM)Formation Aghajari, Gachsaran, Asmari, Pabdeh, GurpiRisks &amp;amp; Hole problems Tight holes, wash &amp;amp; ream (especially in pipe connections), minor stucks and bit balling, Hole Deviation, WahsoutDeviation control Single shot each 100m, multi shot in 300mLogging CDR/GR/CALI (Open hole)GR/CCL/CBL/VDL (Cased Hole)Bit Type 115, 135,537,517, PDC (M1965)Drilling FluidPre-hydrated Bentonite Mud and KCL-Polymer MudMW 67-88pcfPV 5-27Vis. 40-50Drilling ParametersWOB Maximum AvailableRPM 120-180GPM 800-1000

Formation Sampling

Every 10 meters a sample would be to section TD.

NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.

Sequence of Operation

➢ Make up 17-1/2” BHA as per BHA Design. RIH, tag top of cement, Drill out cement &amp;amp; shoe and rat

hole cement. Work on junk sub.

➢ Watch carefully for any indication of abnormal torque, since there might be a possibility of shoe

being backed off. Report problem immediately.

➢ Continue drilling 3m below shoe. Clean out the rat hole, circulate clean.

➢ Drill ahead formation down to 25m below 20” casing shoe with hi-vis pill.

Note:

➢ If use stabilizer to drill out shoe track make sure the RPM &amp;amp; WOB are in low as possible until

the stabilizer is out from shoe Then Cont Drilling 17-1/2” hole section with same BHA.

➢ Circulate and clean hole, POOH to surface and P/U the same bit &amp;amp; S. Stab @ 20m according to

BHA program, RIH to bottom.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 66

➢ Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to 750 m. CBU.

➢ Condition trip to 150m, run back and displace mud in hole by 74 pcf KCL/Polymer.

➢ Continue drilling to 850m or End of Bit Life, circ to clean hole, POOH to surface.

➢ P/U new PDC bit and Drill ahead 17-1/2” hole section down to casing point @ ±2211m, 5m TVD

below top of Gurpi formation meanwhile perform condition trip each 300 m.

Note: In case of observe Cherty zone (Pabdeh formation), Stop Drilling, circulate to clean the hole, POOH to surface and change PDC bit by Tri Cone Bit.

Note:

➢ For this well Single-shot every 100m and multi-shot survey every 300m to Control Well

Deviation or take gyro shot (5-6 shots each 500-600m)

➢ Adjust mud weight according to program and as hole condition dictate.

➢ Tight holes, minor stucks and bit balling are hole problems which happen in offset wells.

➢ TD should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole for probable fillings, before

running casing.

➢ Choke manifold and choke line must be lined up for SOFT SHUT-IN through pit or burning

line.

➢ Ensure that adequate fIIFing tools for this section are available.

➢ Ensure that enough amount and type of LCM are available and keep ready to mix and pump

LCM Pills to cure losses.

➢ At section TD, circulate, clean hole. Perform wiper trip to shoe and observe well @ shoe. RIH, Check

for any fill. CBU on bottom. Spot 100 bbls hi-vis pill @ bottom.

➢ If observed any indication of tight hole, run back to TD. W&amp;amp;R to make sure that the hole condition is

good prior to run Log.

➢ Rig up Wireline logging equipment and run CDR/GR /CAL log as per program, POOH and L/D log

➢ Perform pre-job safety meeting. R/U casing running equipment. Run casing 68#, N-80, BTC with F.

shoe below first joint &amp;amp; F. collar as per casing program while Circ. BTM’s up at shoe with low GPM.

➢ Rig pump efficiency should be check in trip-tank before cement job.

➢ Casing shoe depth should be adjusted in such a way to have 1 meters of rat hole and enough stick

up (1.5 m) on rig floor.

➢ When casing is at casing setting depth circulate while preparing for cementing M/U &amp;amp; test cementing

lines. Circulate for 120% of casing annulus volume.

➢ Cement casing as per program. Any minor adjustment regarding the corrected depth will be done in

due time. Displace with mud. Monitor for pressures.

➢ Bump plug with 1500 psi (Max 80% Casing Burst) / 15 min as CIT (In case of not observing bumping

during CMT job, perform CIT W/1500psi (Max 80% of 13-3/8” Casing Burst)/15min with consider

mud weight after D/O CMT 7m above F. Shoe also consider MW while perform CIT), Record and

report the final pressure, Bleed off pressure, check for back flow, if any back flow observed, hold the

pressure inside casing until the cement get hard.

➢ Hold on casing string weight. Drop casing hanger. Slack off 80% of casing string weight. Test hanger

to 800-1000 psi pressure. (Open base flange side outlet valves while testing).

➢ Remove the flow line and diverter. Rough cut L.J. Dress and bevel casing neck.

➢ Install Casing head spool 20 ¾”-3000*13 ⅝”-5000, Pack p-seals and pressure test p-seals and

cavity with maximum 80% of rated casing collapse pressure. (1800 psi)

➢ Ensure that the casing is drifted; cleaned and tallied priors to RIH.

➢ Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 67

➢ Use Bow Type centralizer each 4 jts in open-hole and at least 2 no. Rigid type centralizer in casedhole. (In shale/sticky Marl layers use bow types each 10 jts)

o Final centralizer arrangement will be issued with final cementing program.

➢ Based on the results obtained from cementing, decision will be made to perform top-up jobs.

➢ Before installing the casing head spool, open all the test ports and clean inside it with water and air.

➢ Check the 13-5/8” wear bushing, 13-5/8” Plug Tester and 9-5/8” Casing Slip in CHS and adopter

flange before installing CHS (on Surface).

➢ N/U 13 5/8” x 10000 psi BOP stack. N/U bell nipple. Test BOP stack and related lines to 8000psi

4.3.3 12-1/4” Hole Section, 9-5/8” Casing

General

Casing Point 9-5/8” Casing @±3942.5m (top of Fahliyan)Formation Gurpi, Ilam, Laffan, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan, GadvanRisks &amp;amp; Hole problems Tight Hole &amp;amp; Stuck, Wash &amp;amp; Ream, Hole Deviation,Deviation control Single shot every 100m, and multi shot every 300mBit Type &amp;amp; Nozzle 214,517, PDC (M1952)LoggingFull Suite logs/ Deviation tool/MDT (Open hole)CBL/VDL/GR/CCL/SBT, USIT, CAST (Cased Hole)Drilling FluidKCL Polymer MudMW 71-85PV 10-25Vis. 45-60Drilling ParametersWOB 15-40RPM 80-160GPM 500-750

Formation Sampling

sample would be taken F/Gurpi to Lafan Every 5 meters &amp;amp; from Sarvak to Gadvan Every 2 Mmd

NOTE: In addition to this general rule, geologist may decide to take more samples.

Sequence of Operations

➢ Make up 12-1/4” BHA as per BHA design include Junk Sub. RIH, Tag top of cement. Drill out plugs,

collar, drill out F. collar and cement to 7m above shoe (In case of observe no bumping during

cementing perform casing integrity test with 1500 psi/15min) drill out cement and shoe track (while RIH

pick up and rack back 500m 5"DP.)

Note: if shoe and float collar are PDC drilable, use PDC bit with stabilizer at first trip with out junk sub and take care while drill shoe track to avoid bit damage.

➢ Drill rat hole to 3-5m below shoe. Clean out the rat hole, circulate clean. Work on junk sub.

➢ POOH to surface.

➢ R/U Logging Equipment, Run GR/CCL/CBL/VDL Log, POOH To Surface. L/D Logging Equipment.

➢ RIH W/12-1/4” PDC Bit and BHA to BTM

➢ Displace hole with new mud ±76 pcf. Perform FIT (3-5m below shoe: EMW (550 psi) should be equal to

max. planned mud weight for next hole section plus 2 pcf),

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 69

➢ Resume DRLG 12-1/4” Hole Section to 3943.5m (9-5/8” CSG point top of Fahliyan) while perform

condition trip each 300 m.

Note: use single shot each 100m and mulri shot each 300m to Control Well Deviation or take gyro shot (5-6 shots each 500-600m)

Note:

➢ Pre-plan for different loss control conditions including LCM materials in defferent sizes &amp;amp; cement plug.

➢ BOP's test every week.

➢ Choke line and choke manifold must be lined up for Soft SHUT-IN.

➢ Adjust mud weight according to nearby wells and as hole condition dictation.

➢ The Section TD maybe changed based on formation tops detected by the wellsite geologist.

➢ The mud engineer shall check the mud properties as pre-planned time schedule.

➢ Ensure that all drilling tools and equipment are on well site in serviceable condition.

➢ Ensure that adequate fIIFing tools are available for this section.

➢ Ensure that enough amounts of LCM are available at location.

➢ At TD, circulate and clean the hole prior to wiper trip. Observe the hole for one hour @ casing shoe.

RIH to bottom and Pump 50 bbl hi-vis pill, circulate to clean hole (check bottoms up for any influx),

Spot 70 bbl hi-vis Pill prior to POOH.

➢ It’s necessary to control drilling fluid rheology and especially solid content. It’s practically proven that

excessive solid percent can reduce ROP significantly and cause major drilling difficulties. So, it’s

necessary to check solid control equipments and shaker screen inventory of the rig prior to drilling

this hole section.

➢ POOH to surface. If observed any indication of drag, run back to bottom. Wash and ream to make

sure that the hole condition is good prior run log.

➢ R/U wireline logging equipment. Run Wire line Loggin (Full Suite logs/ Deviation tool/MDT) as

program. R/D wireline.

➢ RIH and condition hole prior to run casing and spot hi-vis pill.

➢ Change top rams to 9-5/8”.

➢ R/U casing running equipment. Perform pre-job safety meeting.

➢ Use Torque Monitoring System (TMS).

➢ Run casing as per casing program. Run 9-5/8” casing 53.5#, 110SS, Permium Gas tight Connection

with F. shoe below first joint &amp;amp; F. collar, as per casing program while Circ. BTM’s up at shoe with low

GPM.

➢ Rig pump efficiency should be check in trip-tank before cement job.

➢ Casing shoe depth should be adjusted for having short rat hole and enough stick up (1.5 m) on rig

floor. Fill casing by non-LCM fresh mud each 10 joints.

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 70

➢ When casing is at casing setting depth circulate while preparing for cementing M/U &amp;amp; test cementing

lines. Circulate for 120% of casing annulus volume

➢ M/U cementing head. Load the plug. WSS shall witness the loading. Test the lines to 4000psi for 10

minutes. Make sure that the cement head is capable of holding pressure while displacing and testing

cement (Visually).

➢ Perform cementing as per program. Any minor adjustment of cement volume in regards to correction

of depth will be done in due time. Displace with mud. Monitor for pressures. Record and report

bumping pressure in case of observed bumping; perform CIT With 3000psi/15 Min. (in case of

bumping with max 80% casing burst), Record and Report the final pressure. (In case of plug was

not bumped during CMT job, D/O cement to 7m above F. shoe &amp;amp; perform CIT with 3000psi

(Max 80% of 9-5/8” Casing Burst)/ 15min and also consider MW while perform CIT). Bleed off

pressure. Check for back flow. If any back flow observed, hold the pressure inside casing until the

cement get hard.

➢ “Any minor adjustment of cement volume in regards to correction of depth will be done in due time.”

Monitor for pressures. Record and report bumping pressure. Bleed off pressure. Check for back

flow. If any back flow observed, hold the pressure inside casing until the cement is hard.

➢ Hold on casing string weight. Drop casing hanger. Slack off 80% of casing string weight. Test hanger

to 800-1000 psi pressure. (Open side outlet valves while testing).

➢ Raise BOP. Rough cut landing joint. L/D same. Dress and bevel casing neck.

➢ Install T.H.S. 13 5/8”-5000 PSI x 11"–10000 PSI. Pack p‐seals and pressure test p‐seals and cavity

with maximum 80% of rated collapse pressure. (3800 psi)

Note:

➢ Before installing the casing head spool, open all the test ports and clean inside it with water and air.

➢ Use Bow Type centralizer each 3 jts in open-hole and at least 4 no. Rigid type centralizer in casedhole. (In shale/sticky Marl layers use bow types each 10 jts)

➢ During Casing running TMS (Torque Monitoring System) shall be used.

➢ N/U bell nipple. Test BOP stack and related lines to 8000 psi pressure, using plug tester.

➢ L/D 8-1/2” drill collars, P/U &amp;amp; M/U Next BHA and stand drill pipes.

Sequence of Operations

➢ Pick up 8 ½” bit and bit sub and junk sub, RIH and drill out cement and shoe track and shoe.

➢ Drilling 8 1/2" hole (3-5m) to 3946m.

➢ Work on junk sub, Circulate and clean hole, POOH to Surf. L/D 8-1/2” bit &amp;amp; junk sub.

➢ R/U logging equipment, Run GR/CCL/CBL/VDL/SBT, POOH &amp;amp; R/D logging Equip.

➢ P/U &amp;amp; M/U New 8 1/2" PDC bit (Optional performance) and S. Stab @ 20m, RIH to bottom.

➢ Circulate and clean hole, displace hole with new mud (100 pcf KCL Polymer Mud), perform FIT.

(EMW (720 psi) should be equal to max. planned mud weight for 8-1/2” hole section plus 2 PCF),

➢ Drilling 8-1/2” vertical Hole Section to 4200m (7” Liner Point) while use single-shot every 100m and

multi-shot survey every 300m)

➢ Perform Condition trip as pooh to 3943m &amp;amp; run back to BTM, C.B.U, POOH to surface.

➢ R/U logging equipment, Run Full Suite logs/ Deviation tool/MDT, POOH &amp;amp; R/D logging Equip.

➢ R/U casing running equipment. Run and hang 7" liner from ±3862.5 mMD to ±4199mMD, (Hung

±80m above 9 5/8" CSG shoe).

➢ M/U and torque up the shoe-to-shoe joint (32ppf, 110SS, gas tight premium connection. F/U first

joint then P/U &amp;amp; M/U second joint of 7” liner, run &amp;amp; F/U same. Break circulation and function test

shoe.

➢ P/U float Collar, clean threads, M/U and use baker lock for its box. M/U 1 joint 7” liner (32ppf, 110SS,

gas tight premium connection) above float Collar &amp;amp; run &amp;amp; F/U same, break circulation and check

float valve. P/U LDC, clean threads, M/U and use baker lock for its box, add one more 7” liner (32ppf,

110SS, gas tight premium connection), Break circulation and check float valve.

➢ Run 7” liner as per Tally, F/U all 5 first joints, and then F/U every 5 joint. M/U liner hanger assembly

as per job specialist instruction.

➢ Record pick-up and slack-off weight of liner.

➢ RIH 1 stand DP. Break &amp;amp; continue circulation as 1.2 cap of liner string. Check liner hanger for leaks.

➢ R/D liner running equipment

➢ Continue RIH on DP’s while drifting DP’s. F/U each 10 stands. With the liner assembly shoe at 9

5/8” casing shoe, break circulation and check free circulation through the liner assembly. Circulate

at least 1.2 volume of Liner + Drill Pipe capacity.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 74

➢ Continue RIH to liner setting depth, wash down last stand to bottom. Tag bottom. Continue

circulation for at least 120% of the liner and DP’s volume. Space out the string to have cement

manifold at proper position.

➢ Circulation test at following flow rates: 20, 30, 40, 50 SPM, checking for losses, record results in

IADC and advice DSV.

➢ Record pick-up and slack-off weight of liner at bottom.

➢ Drop ball and set liner hanger as per job specialist instruction. Test hanger setting in place. Shear

ball catcher and establIIF circulation. Release setting tool from liner and make sure that it’s free, as

per job specialist procedure.

Note: in case if it is not possible to set liner hanger, set liner shoe in TD then release liner hanger setting tools

➢ Perform Pre-Job safety meeting with all personnel involved on the operation as well as the client

representative. Read and follow the risk assessment for this job, Ensure communication between

cement compant and rig floor

➢ Cement liner as per program. Perform liner integrity test w/ 3000 psi /15 min while cementing (max

80% of liner burst with consider mud weight)

➢ Pump Chemical wash.

➢ Pump spacer.

➢ Pump cement slurry.

➢ Release pump down plug.

➢ Pump 3 bbl cement slurry (as a part of displacement).

➢ Displace cement slurry with mud (decrease flow rate to 4 BPM for last 10 bbl displacement volume).

➢ POOH to Surface while Flush String. L/D setting tools,

Note:

• Check the rig pump efficiency in order to prevent any kind of failure in the cementing job.

• Surge tank, cement unit, batch mixer and dry cement lines should be checked before the

cementing job commencement in presence of well site supervisor, OIM and cement supervisor

to prevent any blockage during the cement job. (Get a confirmation from well site supervisor)

• Drill water, spacer, slurry solution and dry cement samples from each silo should be taken

during the job and sent to lab for analyzing.

• Check the pit for spacer mixing to be clean, to prevent any contamination.

100m above 7" L. Lap, displace hole w/completion Fluid, POOH to surface while L/D All DPs.

➢ Perform BOP test.

➢ Run Completion String as per Program.

4.3.5 Well Flowing

o The main objectives of the 96hrs cleanup and well flowing is to displace completion fluid, possible

mud losses, and flow back the reservoir fluid until achieving the cleanup operation criteria. The

cleanup operation procedure by rig is categorized as follows:

o Rigging up all related-equipment and perform hydro-test

o Retrieving barriers (plug, TWV) and landing protection sleeve in TRSSSV nipple

o Displacing the wellbore via CT with suitable method and compatible fluid

o Flowing the well over 96hrs thru different choke sizes while testing integrity and surface sampling

o Retrieving protection sleeve, inject 5 bbl diesel Close TRSSSV, Set BPV in tubing hanger, secure

well &amp;amp; release rig.

Note: Detaill Clean Up and Well Flowing Program Will Be Issued in Due Time.

7.2 Interval Description

7.2.1 26” Section from Surface to 151 m

Interval Discussion:

Interval Discussion:

The 26-inch section will be drilled to 150 m through Aghajari formation which contains grey Marl and Claystone beds and sandy marl by implementing the Bentonite Mud and Hi-Viscous sweeps in a close system.

This is a large diameter hole, and the drill cuttings removal mechanism will be a combination of high annular velocities and hi-viscous sweeps. The viscous sweep is composed of Pre-Hydrated Gel (PHG). Drill water used as main drilling fluid should be treated with 0.5 ppb Lime. This will minimize dispersion of Marl, Clay cuttings and lower hydration of well bore.

This hole is to be cleaned as required but as a minimum, the following should be adhered to:

• Every single a 20 bbls hi-viscous PHG pill should be pumped to clean the hole.

• At connections, the 50 bbls hi-viscous PHG pill should be spotted at bottom to build filter-cake on the borehole wall, enhance the hole stability and prevent fill up around bit and BHA.

Availability of low chloride water might be a logistical issue and shortage of proper drill water is to be expected, therefore, XC Polymer / CMC pill should be available at the rig site to be used for mixing hi-viscous sweeps. If drilling Water consists of more than 5000mg/lit Chloride and total hardness to be less than 200 mg/lit there would malfunction of Bentonite and TH to be treated as well, therefore, utilizing Polymers could be an option to avoid any delay while drilling. A large volume of fluid will have to be turned over in next section and emphasis will be

placed on pit management and drill water logistics. The engineer should endeavor to have all the pits full of gel based hi-viscous mud to avoid ending up in a “catching up” situation.

Important note: its highly recommended to check the drill water quality prior spudding the well by MIS Ahwaz lab to ensure the mud can be mixed with no issues due to the bad water quality thus a water sample to be provided to MIS by the client.

Hydration and dispersion of dry clay are greatly affected if the makeup water contains salt or various metallic ions. Hence, salinity and total hardness should be checked. The chloride concentration should be less than 5000 mg/l.

At section TD the following procedure shall be adhered to:

• Before pulling out of the hole to run casing the hole shall be swept with a 150 bbls PHG viscous pill and

spotted at least 70 m bottom of open Hole with same PHG viscous pill.

To control mud weight, beside utilizing solid control equipment, a dump and dilute strategy (with Lime treated drill water) should be considered.

Potential mud related problems and hazards

Hole cleaning:

- Pump hi-viscous mud more frequently and of greater volume. - Increase pump rate.

Down Hole Loss:

- Enough suggested volume of LCM pill to be mixed and prepared in advance.

- Sufficient Stock of LCM materials should be stored on the rig site as contingency materials.

Mud composition

Main Fluid Composition: Gel / Bentonite Mud

Drill water : Base fluid

Caustic Soda : Alkalinity Control

Soda Ash : Hardness control / Alkalinity control

Bentonite : Viscosifier

Mixing Procedure:

1- Fill the tanks with the required volume of drill water.

2- Treat out any hardness greater than 400 ppm with soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)

3- Raise the pH to 10 with additions of 0.5 lb/bbl Caustic Soda.

4- Treat out any hardness greater than 200 ppm with soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)

5- Add 15 – 20 lb/bbl Bentonite and allow hydrating if possible. Processing with a shearing device will improve yield.

6- Add Lime to control pH while drilling.

PHG sweep Composition:

Drill water : Base Fluid

Caustic soda / Lime : Alkalinity control

Soda Ash : Hardness control / Alkalinity control

Bentonite : Viscosifier

Interval II

Interval Discussion

The 17 ½” section will be drilled from 20” casing shoe from 150 m through Aghajari formation which contains grey Marl and Claystone with thin bed of Sandstone by implementing Bentonite Mud and Hi-Viscous PHG sweeps to 800 m, then well will be displaced with 74 pcf Salt Polymer Mud to casing depth around 2211 m.

PHG sweeps can be effectively used to clean the hole as per previous section practice in the first part of drilling to 800m, as drilling progressed and while drilling with NaCl/KCl polymer mud, polymer Hi-vis pill will be utilized.

In second parts of fluid, Salt is used primarily as a soluble weight agent. Nevertheless, the increased salinity of the system can also provide a certain degree of inhibition against the swelling clays. At about +/-800m Potassium chloride at initial 1% will provide an extra inhibition when introduced into the mud however, based on the formation behavior can be maintained and increased to 3% which will provide more inhibition against swelling and bit balling while drilling sticky formations such as Marl and Clay. Salt also can be used to reduce dispersion and hydration of clays.

For more inhibition it is recommended using of 0.5 ppb PHPA for removing free clay and marl cuttings from the mud system. PHPA will be incorporated into a fresh batch of mud preferably from the reserve pits with reduced hardness (&amp;lt;300mg/lit). This could be pumped about 25 bbl pill every stand drilled or as per the hole dictates to be decide by the mud engineer and company rep.

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 92

Its highly recommended to maintain Drilling Detergent in the salt polymer mud from the start at 0.2 ppb in order to minimize bit balling and accretion while drilling marl formation.

As per our experience in Azadegan oil field, pumping DD brine at the end of each stand drilled is more effective to reduce the risk of Bit Balling during drilling this interval.

Fluid Loss in spud pill should be maintained at below 3cc/30min, Percentage of KCl should be 5% and also mud weight should be designed 2 pcf more than active mud weight. The spud mud volume is about 250bbl or to cover 250m of drilled hole.

Recommended sweeps regime from 153 to 800 meters:

Every single a 20 bbls hi-viscous pill should be pumped to clean the hole.

At connections, the 40 bbls hi-viscous pill should be spotted on bottom in order to build filter-cake on the borehole wall, enhance the hole stability and prevent fill up around bit and BHA.

Potential Mud Related Problems and Mitigation Action:

Hole Cleaning:

This is a large diameter hole, and the drill cuttings removal mechanism will be a combination of high annular velocities and hi-viscous sweeps. The viscous sweep is composed of Pre-Hydrated Gel (PHG) before 800 m. after this depth, Hi-vis pill would be designed with second interval fluid which is Salt Polymer mud. Viscous pill will be made by XC-Polymer or CMC HV to clean the hole to casing depth as per well profile.

Hole would be swept by Hi-Vis pills if any sign of cutting accumulation in annulus (e.g., drag, torque or pressure increase) been observed. The recommended volume is 20-30bbls each time. If situation dictates pills should be dumped on bottom’s up to prevent excessive viscosity and solid content of mud.

This hole is to be cleaned as required but as a minimum, the following should be adhered to:

Every middle of each single a 15 bbls hi-viscous pill should be pumped to clean the hole.

At connections, the 35 bbls hi-viscous pill should be spotted on bottom to build filter-cake on the bore-hole wall, enhance the hole stability and prevent fill up around bit and BHA.

At 800 m the following procedure shall be adhered to displace well with Salt/KCl Polymer Mud:

Before displacing, the hole shall be swept with 100 bbls PHG viscous pill then, 50 bbl of 70 pcf Polymer Pill should be pumped. At the end of displacing job, in one stage all mud in hole would be displaced with Salt Polymer Mud.

In case of using “wastewater”, a pilot test should be carried out by mud engineer to check same with fifty percent of drill water to prevent any malfunction of bentonite. Otherwise, MIS won’t be responsible about extra using chemical to adjust/keep mud properties in specified range.

Down hole losses:

There is a potential loss zone, and it is recommended to have LCM pill available before drilling through that zone. The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses volume. Sufficient stocks of graded LCM should be kept at location.

In case of seepage losses, the system should be treated system with a finer grade of LCM at 10 – 15 lb/bbl. In the event of moderate to high rate of well bore loss, prepare pills at different graded concentrations 20 -40 ppb and spot over thief zone.

The concentration may vary between 20 – 70 lb/bbl. Ensure that the driller displace the LCM pill out of the string prior to shutting down the mud pumps. In the event of severe losses, cross-linking pills should be pumped to minimize losses.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 93

Avoid cuttings accumulation in annulus by proper shakers monitoring and sweep regime as unnecessary higher mud weight and flow rate since can cause higher ECD in the annulus which can result in induce losses.

Mud system can be pre-treated with medium and coarse calcium carbonate prior to reaching the possible loss zones as background LCM and bridging strategy. Since the material could be easily filtered out over the shakers, replenishment these products would be required. A typical addition rate can be one 55 lb sack of each size per hour into the active system unless hole became completely steady and no loss and gain prevails.

Solid &amp;amp; Mud Weight control

The initial weight will be 65 pcf for the section allow time for the cold mud to warm up and shear to avoid excessive ECD. With this weight the fluid system will be above the collapse pressure of the well bore and provide bore stability. That is valid unless the hole condition dictates otherwise. Controlling of MW could be done by running all SCE and dressing the shakers with finest mesh screens &amp;amp; dumping the sand trap and dilution approximately each 90 m drilled.

After displacing well with Salt Polymer Mud, all Shaker screens should be changed to finer screen and all solid control equipment should be run to control the LGS. In the higher Mud Weight should be run both centrifuges as supper clone mode.

Rheology Control

Dilution with fresh mud and/or premixes should be made periodically to ensure good rheological properties and minimize solids build-up; Rheology should be maintained in specification to minimize the effects of ECD on the formation. The mud engineer should be monitoring the shakers and ensure sufficient hole cleaning would be provided by drilling fluids based on the ROP.

Stuck Pipe and Tight Spots

Pipe Lax pill can be spotted around stuck area to free the pipe.

possible tight spots might be experienced due to marl and clay from formation, its recommended to control the fluid loss recommended range at +/- 800 meters and ensure NaCl brine will be added to help inhibition.

Influx

Monitoring mud properties and report any sudden changes in the chlorides, hardness at BTM’s up.

Bit balling and Hole instability

Bit Balling can occur due to the soft formation that contains clay and is attributed to different factors other than mud chemistry, such as bit hydraulics. At times, hydraulics is not adequate to clean the bit and stabilizer balling might occurred. If hydraulics appears to be adequate, then a strategy of pumping cleaning pills (such as Drilling Detergent and walnut and/or caustic wash pills) might be considered to minimize bit balling.

Its highly recommended to maintain drilling detergent in the mud from the start at 0.2 ppb to minimize bit balling while drilling marl formation.

Alternatively, with each stand drilled, a 25 bbl of saturated salt/DD pill (2.5%V/V) can be pumped and circulated out to minimize bit balling and accretion.

Increased concentration of Drilling Detergent can effectively assist tackle or reduce the bit balling. The usage should be confirmed by company man.

Using defoamers might be necessary to tackle foaming. In case of low ROP pump down fresh brine treated with caustic soda. Abrasive substances such as mica or nut plug can help better cleaning of the BHA in a turbulent flow regime.

Funnel Viscosity : &amp;gt; 60 sec/qt.

“OR”

Mixing Procedure:

Treat out any hardness greater than 200 ppm with 1 lb/bbl Soda ash. (Based on Drill Water Properties)

Note: The KCl concentration should be adjusted after pumping Hi-Vis pill and during drilling continuously.

Solid Control Equipment:

Shakers:

After Displacing well with salt/KCl Polymer Mud, all shale shaker screens should be changed based on flow condition and ROP. It is highly recommended to reduce the cutting retention time to prevent of any screen plugging and damaging accordingly.

Mud cleaner which shall be dressed in API 325 to remove LGS while running Desilter and Desander.

Note: Each shaker should be dressed just with same API size, and it is meaningless to dress a shaker with different mesh sizes.

Objective:

This section will be drilled through Gurpi, Tarbur, Ilam, Sarvak, Kazhdumi, Dariyan and Gadvan formation and few meters into Fahliyan formation.

Interval Discussions:

This section will be drilled by Limestone, KCl polymer (Glycol) mud with the range of 71 to 85 pcf. Drilling fluid for 12 1/4” hole section will be prepared from scratch.

Fluid loss should be controlled with Starch LV and Pac LV. The rheological properties will enhance by implementing of CMC-HV or Xanthan Gum. KCl and Salt will inhibit the shale swelling and PAC will encapsulate the Shale cuttings in this section. Also, high concentration of Chlorite, generated from NaCl addition will cause to minimize Water Activity with accordance to the formation water activity in order to create more inhibition while drilling Shale layers out.

Glycol will help the hole instability to prevent any shale swelling by providing of micro emulsion process.

Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

Hole instability: Make sure mud properties are in range and mud weight is adequate to keep well bore stability and prevent tight hole. KCl and Glycol are shale inhibitors and are consumed in this section to provide a full inhibition.

Lost circulation: The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Sufficient stocks of graded LCM should be kept at location. In case of seepage losses, the system should be treated with fine grade of LCM at 25 - 30 ppb, for moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and spot across the thief zone.

Mud Composition: Salt saturated / KCl polymer / Limestone mud

Drill Water : Base fluid

KCl : Swelling Inhibitor

Caustic Soda : pH control

Soda Ash : Hardness control

XC-Polymer : Viscosifier / Rheology improver

Starch : Fluid loss control

Pac LV : Fluid loss control

Limestone : Weighting agent

NaCl : Weighting agent (Inhibitor)

Glycol (Optional) : Shale Inhibitor

Recommended Properties:

Mud weight – pcf : 71-85

Yield Point – lb/100ft2 : 10 - 18

pH : 9.5-10.5

LGS – % : &amp;lt; 7

API fluid loss : &amp;lt; 4

Chloride – gr/l : 180-185

MBT-ppb : &amp;lt; 10

KCl %w : 3 – 5%

Glycol (Optional) : 1 – 3%

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 99

Engineering Comments

KCl Content: If cuttings integrity and well condition dictate, increase KCl content up to 5 % by weight.

Losses: surface and sub surface losses to be monitored, prepare LCM pill in advance.

Density: Drilling fluid density will be controlled by addition of Limestone. Use available solids control equipment to reduce low gravity solids build-up, thus reducing any dilution requirements for density control. Mud weight to be increased as hole conditions dictate.

Yield Point: will be adjusted by adding rheology modifier and control the amount of LGS and MBT.

Fluid loss control, API: The API fluid loss should be kept within specifications using additions of PAC LV and Starch LV.

CEC: To be controlled with solids control equipment and dilution.

Hole cleaning: Hole should be regularly checked and swept by pumping Hi-Vis pill at the end of each stand drilled.

Solid control: Ensure shakers are running immediately prior to and during all periods of circulation. Shakers to be dressed initially with 100/120 mesh screens and after the mud is sheared, finer screens of 140/170 mesh should be dressed. Use centrifuges as required to control MW and LGS.

Dump and dilute strategy: Dilute active system with premixed fluid to maintain volume and mud properties. The concentration of this fluid should be somewhat higher than in the initial mix to compensate depletion.

Bacterial decay: before any pull out of hole, treat mud system with biocide to prevent bacterial decay.

Foaming: drilling Anhydrite may cause foam due to flocculation of polymer and air trapping. Treat system with Soda ash and defoamer to treat it.

Interval Discussions

For drilling 8 ½" interval, a KCl Polymer WBM system will be used and previous mud should be transferred and weighted up to use in this interval.

The water-based KCL Polymer system provides effective inhibition when drilling reactive shales/clay through Below mechanism:

Potassium Chloride provides a source of Potassium ions, which are small enough to fit between the clay platelets without distorting the shale lattice. The Potassium ions are adsorbed on the exchangeable cation sites in the shale lattice and this holds the platelets together, which helps to minimize clay and shale hydration, swelling and dispersion. This system is used primarily to drill moderately reactive shales.

Potential Mud Related Problems and Hazards.

Well bore and formation related problems are identified as follows:

• Potential losses

• Differential sticking

• Bottom Hole temperature

Stuck Pipe: Differential pressure stuck pipe may occur due to the different gradient between formation and mud. If stuck pipe occur, Pipe Lax spot should be pumped according with standard Company spotting procedures.

Lost circulation: The concentration of LCM will vary according to losses. Partial to severe losses are and sufficient stocks of LCM should be kept on board. In case of seepage losses, the system should be treated with fine grade of LCM at 10 ppb, for moderate to severe losses, prepare pills at different graded concentrations and spot across the thief zone.

In order to avoid LCM plugging MWD tools a circulation sub should be installed in the drill string. The MWD engineer is to advise on the size and concentration of LCM that can be pumped through MWD Tools. Ensure that the drilling displace the entire LCM pill out of the string prior to shutting down the mud pumps.

Bottom Hole Temperature: Starch HT is recommended to utilize in this section to prevent any fermentation.

Mud Composition

KCl/ Salt/ Polymer

Fresh water : 400 mg/l Calcium.

Caustic Soda : pH control.

Soda Ash : Hardness control.

XC-Polymer : Rheological control extender

Starch HT : Fluid Loss Control

PAC-LV : Fluid loss control

DEFOAMER : Anti Foam

CaCO3 Fine : Weighting agent

KCl : Shale Inhibitor

Mixing Procedure:

Mixing procedure for preparing main mud:

• Fill the tanks with the required volume of fresh-water.

• Add KCl at 18-26 ppb (3%)

• Add NaCl till reaching to total chloride concentration at 180 g/l.

• Raise the pH to 9.5-10.5 with additions of caustic soda.

• Treat out any hardness greater than 600 mg/l with Soda Ash.

• Add Starch HT at 12-14 ppb.

• Add PAC-LV at 2 -4 ppb.

• Add XC-Polymer at 1 - 2 ppb.

• Add Defoamer at required concentration and mix properly.

• Add CaCO3 fine to adjust MW at the desired mud weight.

Recommended Properties:

Mud weight (pcf) : 100 - 106

Yield Point (lb/100 ft2) : 12 - 26

3 rpm reading : 5 - 7

6 rpm reading : 7 - 10

pH : 9.5 – 10.5

API fluid loss (ml/30 min : &amp;lt; 4

Gel, 10s ( lb/100ft2 ) : 5 - 7

Gel, 10m ( lb/100ft2 ) : 7 - 9

MBT (lb/bbl) : &amp;lt;10

KCl : 3 %

PHPA (Optional) : 0.25

Waste Management General Plan Introduction

The waste water and drilling fluids waste treatment is the main important part of the services that should be consider more to minimize the waste treatment cost. In addition, by recycling the water phase of the waste, we reduce the water consumption and recover soluble chemicals. This reduces the cost of material consumptions and the cost of dumping waste water at site. This process is usually done by using a chemically mechanically method to separate drilling solids and recycle water. This process is usually done by using a high-speed centrifuges or filtering equipment and chemical dosing system. This process is very efficient in drilling site especially in locations that water supply is the main problem. Chemical Precipitation is the most common technology used to remove the dissolved particles from solutions. The ionic particles are converted to an insoluble form (particle) by the chemical reaction between the soluble compounds and the precipitating reagent. The particles formed by this reaction are removed from solution by settling, filtration or other separation equipment. The unit operations typically required in this technology includes neutralization, precipitation, coagulation/ flocculation, solids/liquid separation.

Cutting’s treatment is important part of the waste management services which is usually done to solidification and stabilize and fix the liquid phase movement on the solids. In this treatment, material is added to reduce free water and possibly reduce or slow potential contaminant release. The treated cuttings will be transferred to environmentally designated area specified by client.

All related services such as design, supply, execute, supervise and control of waste water and waste mud treatment plan was performed according to the industry standards. The above-mentioned waste treatment operations performed under and complied with Iranian Petroleum Standards (IPS) as well as good engineering practices in oil and exploration industry.

Drilling Waste Management Process

Waste Management Services Policy

PDF offers a wide range of waste management services to minimize generated waste and contaminated discharged cuttings during drilling operations. This helps keep the environment clean and ensures that our operations are in compliance with DOE (Department of Environment) legislations.

We provide cost-effective and optimum solutions from environmentally acceptable procedures to achieve zero-discharge plans and improve fluids recovery. The outcome reduces operational costs and enables recycling.

Wastes are analyzed for various parameters such as chloride, nitrate, phosphate, BOD (Biological Oxygen Demand), COD (Chemical Oxygen Demand) and oil content. Other analysis is carried out to monitor treatment processes on a regular basis during waste generation. Our waste management personnel are fully trained, experienced and have the competencies in place to provide the best reliable services with concentrated support and the least liabilities.

▪ Waste Water

Drilling Cuttings Treatment

Cuttings Treatment Process

Cutting’s treatment is important part of the waste management services which is usually done to solidification and stabilize and fix the liquid phase movement on the solids.

Solidification refers to techniques that encapsulate the waste in a monolithic solid of high structural integrity. In this treatment, material is added to reduce free water and possibly reduce or slow potential contaminant release. Contaminant migration is restricted by vastly decreasing the surface area exposed to leaching by isolating the wastes within an impervious capsule.

Stabilization refers to those techniques that reduce the hazard potential of wastes by converting contaminants into their least soluble, mobile, or toxic form. The physical nature and handling characteristics of the waste are not necessarily changed by stabilization.

Flocculation Unit

Chemical Precipitation is the most common technology used to remove the dissolved particles from solutions. The ionic particles are converted to an insoluble form (particle) by the chemical reaction between the soluble compounds and the precipitating reagent. The particles formed by this reaction are removed from solution by settling, filtration or other separation equipment. The unit operations typically required in this technology includes neutralization, precipitation, coagulation/ flocculation, solids/liquid separation. 1- Chemical Mixing Area

2- Chemical Dosing Unit

3- Control Unit

Bridging Flocculation

Polymer chains can "bridge" particles to create larger masses that settle out

Charge Neutralization

Like charged particles repel removal of charge can enable particles to approach close enough to coagulate

Laboratory Test Kits

Spectrophotometry

Spectrophotometry uses photometers that can measure a light beam's intensity as a function of its color (wavelength) known as spectrophotometers. Important features of spectrophotometers are spectral bandwidth, (the range of colors it can transmit through the test sample), and the percentage of sampletransmission, and the logarithmic range of sample-absorption and sometimes a percentage of reflectance measurement.

A spectrophotometer is commonly used for the measurement of transmittance or reflectance of solutions, transparent or opaque solids, such as polIIFed glass, or gases

Conductivity Meter

The common laboratory conductivity meters employ a potentiometric method and four electrodes. Often, the electrodes are cylindrical and arranged concentrically. The electrodes are usually made of platinum metal. An alternating current is applied to the outer pair of the electrodes. The potential between the inner pair is measured. Conductivity could in principle be determined using the distance between the electrodes and their surface area using the Ohm’s Law but generally, for accuracy, a calibration is employed using electrolytes of well-known conductivity.

pH Meter

A pH Meter is a scientific instrument that measures the hydrogen-ion concentration (or pH) in a solution, indicating its acidity or alkalinity. The pH meter measures the difference in electrical potential between a pH electrode and a reference electrode. Knowledge of pH to greater or lesser accuracy is useful or critical in a great many situations, including of course chemical laboratory work. pH meters of various types and quality can be used for soil measurements in agriculture; water quality for water supply systems, swimming pools, etc.; brewing, industrially or domestically; healthcare, to ensure that solutions are safe when applied to patients or lethal as storylands and disinfectants; and many other applications. It has measurements from 0 to 14

Titration Method

Titration, also known as titrimetric, is a common laboratory method of quantitative chemical analysis that is used to determine the unknown concentration of an identified analyze. Since volume measurements play a key role in titration, it is also known as volumetric analysis. A reagent, called the titrant or titration is prepared as a standard solution. A known concentration and volume of titrant reacts with a solution of analyze or titrant to determine concentration. The volume of titrant reacted is called titration volume

Summary:

• Cement of the well is part of the well integrity barrier envelope. In addition, cement protects the casing

from corrosion due to potential formation fluids on the outside well.

• Cementing in the 17 ½” section returns to surface has historically been a challenge. This is because

of the formation strength below the 20” surface casing is not sufficient to bring cement all them way to

the surface.

• Cementing the 12 ¼” section is as equally challenging as for the 17 ½” section, due to small limit

between pore pressure and fracture pressure.

• 20” surface casing will be set @±151m. Cemented up to surface till return to surface (cellar floor).

• 13 ⅜" intermediate casing will be set @±2210m. Cemented up to cellar floor. 40% excess on slurries

for open hole-Casing volume &amp;amp; 20% excess on slurries for casing-casing volume.

• 9 ⅝" production casing will be set @±3942.5m. It will be cemented up to cellar floor. 40% excess on

slurries for open hole-Casing volume &amp;amp; 20% excess on slurries for casing-casing volume.

• 7" liner will be set @ ±4199 mMD. It will be cemented up to liner lap @ ±3862.5 m (80 m overlap on

production CSG). 50% excess on slurries for open hole-Casing volume &amp;amp; 20% excess on slurries for

casing-casing volume.

General Procedure:

Prior to each cement job, the unit will be checked accurately. Fluid ends, engines, etc.

The lines will have to be flushed with the mixing water. Cementing heads, plugs will be inspected before the job.

The plugs will be inserted into the cementing head in presence of the Drilling Operation Representative.

Bumping the plug is MUST.

The cement density will be checked, using pressurized balance.

Volume calculations &amp;amp; displacement will be done by &amp;amp; cement engineer.

Rig pump should be used to displace the cement.

Important: The following program is a sample; therefore, it should be tested using the field drilling water, cement sample and additives.

11.2 Material Selection

Casing

Corrosive Environment Analysis and Strength Check

Based on the data of offset wells in this oilfield, there is a certain degree of corrosive problems in the 4 layers to be produced. Corrosive medium is principally composed of 2 parts: one is the corrosive gases like H2S and CO2 existing in the produced fluid; and the other is the high salinity formation water. The contents of main corrosive fluids in offset wells are shown in Table

H2S and CO2 Content

1) Corrosion in this oilfield is a result of combined action of H2S, CO2 and high salinity formation water.

The corrosive mechanism is complicated, and the corrosive rate on the casing is difficult to

determine. Based on the NACE standard and the data from the Table, the producers result in severe

corrosive environment. However, by the research on development conditions and technical indices,

it can be found that although the corrosive fluid contents are high in the oilfield, the corrosive

environment is not extremely serious as the following reasons:

2) In general, the temperature that cause the SSC/SCC is concentrated in the range from 20℃ to 30℃.

The average depth of the horizontal section is 2,730m with the reservoir temperature of 96℃, and

tensional stress on the casing does not exist in the horizontal section. Therefore, the influence of

SSC/SCC may not be considered for the casing/liner.

3) Based on the RMDP, the production GOR in the first development phase should not be higher than

the initial GOR, therefore, the flowing pressure is higher than the bubble point pressure all the time,

that is, degassing problems do not exist in the horizontal section and some vertical section, the H2S

and CO2 have no chance of separation out of oil phase, and it is lack of prerequisite for casing

corrosion.

4) 4Based on above all, the corrosive environment of the casing string is not severe. The optimal

selection for the casing string can be stated briefly as follows

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 175

Material Selection of Casing String

1) In this oilfield, the corrosion of production string obviously occurs in 2 regions. One is the region where the pressure is above the bubble point; in which degassing problems do not exist, and the corrosion environment is relatively weak. Another one is the region where the pressure is below the bubble point; because of the decline of pressure, the concentration of corrosive gas will increase and the corrosion is relatively serious. Considering the corrosive environment is not serious in the low water cut production stage and the production practice in this oilfield has proved the L80 is fit for it, So, the L80 should be taken as the material of 9-5/8″ casing, 7″, 5 1/2″ and 4- 1/2″ liner. In order to prevent the hazard of gas leakage after production degassing occurs, 9-5/8″ casing, 7″ and 4-1/2″ liner should be Gas tight thread. 2) The 110S is selected as the material of 9-5/8″/7″/4-1/2″ casing rather than L-80 due to the high strength requirement in Fahliyan wells, what’s more the 110S is fit for sour gas environment
