20 -3/4" x 13- 5/8" x 11" x 4- 1/16" x 10,000 psi WP for Fahliyan wellsSection ACasing head housing for 20” (94lb/ft) OD casing x 20 3/4” 3,000 psi API flange has hold down bolts for wear bushing, with 2 x 2- 1/16” 3000 psi studded side outlets, test ports and seals. One side outlet has one 2 1/16”x 3,000 psi manual gate valve, 2” LP threaded companion flange and 2” bull plug threaded for ½” NPT and fitted with 0-3,000 psi gauge and needle valve and, the other side to have 2 1/16” x 3,000 psi x 2” LP threaded flange and 2” bull plug. Casing slip and seal assembly self-energizing to hang 13 3/8” (68lb/ft) OD casing. This section is installed on 20” casing stub by way of slip lock with landing plate on 30" conductor pipe. Material DD-0.5, PSL-2, PR-2, Temp U. Height: 630mmMaterial: Forged alloy steel 4130Automatic casing slip material: Low alloy steel 4130Section BCasing head spool with 13 5/8” 5,000 psi top flange x 20 3/4” 3,000 psi bottom flange has hold down bolts for wear bushing and bottom preparation to seal on 13 3/8” OD casing with test ports to test annular space and casing seals. One side outlet has one 2 1/16”x 5,000psi manual gate valve, 2” LP threaded companion flange and 2” bull plug on one side and, the other side has one 2 1/16”x 5,000psi manual gate valve, 2” LP threaded companion flange, 2” bull plug threaded for ½” NPT and fitted with 0- 5,000psi gauge and needle valve. Casing slip and seal assembly self-energizing to hang 9 5/8” (47lb/ft) OD casing. Material DD-0.5, PSL-2, PR-2, Temp U. Height: 665mmMaterial: Forged low alloy steel 413013 5/8”x 9 5/8” automatic casing slip material: Low alloy steel 4130Section CTubing head spool with 11” 10,000 psi top flange x 13 5/8” 5,000 psi bottom flange has hold down bolts for wear bushing and bottom preparation to seal on 9 5/8” OD casing with test ports to test annular space and casing seals C/W T.D.Screws with 410SS material Side outlets have 1.900" sharp vee VR threads on both sides, to have two 2 1/16”x 10,000psi manual gate valve, 2”ACME 10K female thread companion flange, 2” bull plug threaded for ½”NPT and fitted with 0-10,000psi gauge and needle valve on one side and, the other side to have the first 2 1/16”x 10,000psi manual gate valve, 2 1/16" 10,000 psi instrument flange installed with 0ne ½”NPT and fitted with 0-10,000psi gauge and needle valve, and the second 2 1/16" x 10,000 psi manual gate valve, 2” ACME 10K female thread companion flange and 2” bull plug.(manual G.valve to be non-rising stem w/back seat capability). Clear bore for passing 8-1/2" BHA.The 11" x 4 1/2" tubing hanger has 4 1/2" 18.9ppf premium conn. Top &amp;amp; bottom thread one continuous 1/4" NPT control line exit for SSSV, two channels for 3/8" chemical injection line exit, one 1/4" line exit for DHPG. 4" type "H" BPV thread preparation, one metal seal &amp;amp; two S-SEALS to pack off the upper neck and two S-SEAL to pack off the body of hanger, and tonging preparation on the lower neck. Selection of PSL (product specification level)

According to API 6A 19th, The final PSL for wellhead and X-mas tree are as below:

PSL for wellhead and X-mas tree

Item SpecificationCasing Head Housing assembly PSL-1Casing spool assembly PSL-1Tubing spool assembly PSL-3Tubing hanger PSL-3GX-Tree assembly PSL-3G

Selection of PR (Performance Requirement)

According to API Specification 6A 19th edition, performance requirements are specific nd unique to the product in the as-shipped condition. All products shall be designed to perform according to the requirements of API 6A clauses 4 and clauses 10 while in the pressure and temperature ranges and corrosion envirement. Other requirements include load capability, cycles, and operating force or torque. There are two performance requirement levels: PR1 and PR2. Valves operating as safety valves shall be of the PR2 performance level and meet the requirements of Annex I in API 6A For SA wells, the guideline for PR selection is that all parts not contacting well fluids in nomal production period are PR1, all parts contacting well fluids are PR2 as follow, Casing head housing and casing head spool assembly - PR1. Tubing head spool, tubing hanger and X-mas tree - PR2.

14.1 General Quality, Health, Safety and Environmental Requirements

➢ PEDEC is committed to conducting operations in a manner which minimizes the risks to the

health and safety of those involved in, and affected by, such operations. PEDEC is also

committed to conducting its operations in a manner which minimizes the impact on

natural and ‘built’ environments in the area of operations.

➢ This section sets out minimum Quality, Health, Safety and Environmental (QHSE) requirements

for the WORK that TDDC and subcontractors will be required to comply with whilst

conducting operations for PEDEC.

➢ PEDEC may perform audits and assessments of TDDC’s and its subcontractors’ performance

under the CONTRACT. PEDEC reserves the right without prior notice to conduct quality,

health, safety, security and environmental audits of the operations.

➢ TDDC shall at all times be responsible for performing the WORK in a manner so as to protect

against loss of life, injury to people, damage to property and the environment. The detailed

manner means and methods of performing the WORK shall be under the control and direction

of TDDC.

➢ Prior to the start of Operations, certain QHSE processes must be completed. These QHSE

processes include developing the final QHSE plan format and frequency of any QHSE audits,

permitting and licensing and PEDEC’s approvals. TDDC shall ensure that sufficient resources

are dedicated to this task to ensure completion prior to mobilization.

➢ PEDEC may at any time stop the WORK or any portion of the WORK without penalty to

PEDEC if the WORK is not conducted in accordance with the Quality, Health, Safety or

Environmental guidelines specified in this CONTRACT or by the laws of the appropriate

jurisdiction. PEDEC reserves the right to require TDDC to remove any person from the

operations in the case of non-compliance with QHSE procedures. PEDEC may also prohibit

the use of equipment and plant in cases where that equipment or plant does not meet the

required standards or is deemed unsafe.

➢ TDDC shall ensure that activities associated with this CONTRACT are conducted in compliance

with applicable national and local HSE laws and regulations, TDDC’s standards and PEDEC’s

requirements and IADC documents.

➢ Limits of Authority

➢ PEDEC’s Representative(s) do not have the authority to relax health, safety or environment

standards. If they judge the operation to be inadequate with regards to any aspect of quality,

health, safety, security or the environment, then the operation may be immediately suspended.

➢ QHSE Plan

➢ TDDC shall be required to develop a project-specific QHSE plan to manage the WORK and

associated risks. This plan shall, as a minimum, address topics included in the list detailed in

Item 7. Where existing TDDC QHSE publications detail the topics listed, it is sufficient to either

include them verbatim, or provide reference to where they may be found in TDDC’s QHSE

Manuals. The final project-specific QHSE plan shall satisfy TDDC standards, PEDEC

requirements and applicable HSE laws and regulations.

➢ TDDC should establIIF an effective HSE system and implement it strictly.

➢ TDDC must adhere to the standards and regulations of Health, Safe and Environment

constituted by the local government.

➢ TDDC must strictly adhere to the procedures / standards and regulations of Health, Safety and

Environment promulgated by NIOC.

➢ TDDC should comply with all applicable local government regulations including guidelines and

recommended practices and any other Iranian authorities having jurisdiction.

➢ All personnel should respect the local culture, traditions and social values of the indigenous

population of the Iran and South Azadegan region.

➢ TDDC shall hire qualified and experienced worker and familiar his personnel with hazards

especially with H2S and Mine and UXO (unexploded ordinance) according to related procedure.

➢ TDDC shall conduct proper training about UXO (unexploded ordinance) and mine awareness

to all personnel

Health &amp;amp; Safety Management

➢ TDDC shall take all necessary safety measures in relation to the WORK to be provided and shall

conduct itself and its work force in such a way as to comply at all times with the provisions of the

national and local health and safety regulations pertinent to the contracted Scope of WORK.

➢ TDDC shall also comply with all and any of PEDEC’S safety standards and requirements as

particularly applicable to the Operating Area and/or as may be imposed by PEDEC from time to

time.

➢ TDDC and its subcontractors shall take all necessary steps to provide a safe and healthy working

environment for employees, PEDEC’s personnel and third parties in the performance of the

WORK.

➢ TDDC shall operate a management system that ensures:

➢ TDDC’s and sub contractors’ personnel operate safe systems of work at all times for the

performance of the WORK.

➢ Hazards associated with the work are identified and appropriate actions are taken to

prevent accidents.

➢ Risks to safety and health of personnel involved in the WORK are identified and assessed, and

any actions and measurements are implemented to either eliminate or reduce these risks to

a level that is considered as low as reasonably practicable, given the nature of the WORK.

➢ Personnel are aware of the identified hazards and risks to their safety and health and to the

safety and health of others. The detailed safe working procedures establIIFed by TDDC to ensure

that the WORK will be conducted with due regard to the QHSE aspects, shall be detailed or

referenced in the QHSE plan. Copies of the source documents shall be provided to PEDEC if

the procedures are referenced only.

➢ QHSE Representative

TDDC shall provide a dedicated, on-crew QHSE Representative. The primary responsibility of the QHSE Representative shall include, but not be limited to, the maintenance and monitoring of the implementation of HSE guidelines and procedures and where required provide HSE awareness training for the crew. Such activities shall be approved and actively supported by TDDC at corporate management level.

➢ Training and Competence

TDDC shall ensure that its employees and those of its subcontractors are trained and competent

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

to perform the WORK in a safe and environmentally responsible manner. Such training and demonstration of competence will include but not be limited to vocational, safety, emergency, environmental and other training required by regulatory agencies and IADC/OGP guidelines. TDDC’s senior staff shall have received training in safety management.

➢ Accident/Incident Reporting

➢ TDDC shall report all incidents. Any hazardous incident/near miss involving PEDEC, TDDC’s,

or any third-party personnel, plant or equipment, shall be reported to PEDEC, whether or not

injury to personnel or damage/loss to plant or equipment resulted. The following incidents shall

be verbally reported immediately to the on-site PEDEC Representative(s): damage to property

or equipment belonging to PEDEC, third parties, TDDC or its subcontractors; injury to

personnel of PEDEC, third parties, subcontractors or TDDC; near misses which may have had

serious potential consequences; environmental damage; media attention. An initial written

report, containing factual information only, shall be prepared by TDDC and delivered to the

PEDEC Representative within twenty-four (24) hours of the incident.

➢ PEDEC reserves the right to participate in the investigation of any incident arising out of

performance of the WORK.

➢ TDDC and all subcontractors shall have an accident and incident reporting system, details of

which shall be included in the QHSE plan.

➢ TDDC shall maintain and forward to PEDEC, a monthly summary of its safety performance.

➢ TDDC shall prepare and forward to PEDEC, a weekly report stating all incidents and near

misses that occurred during that week.

➢ All TDDC and subcontractor personnel and PEDEC Representative shall attend safety

meetings on a regular basis. PEDEC Representative shall be given a copy of the minutes of

the meetings.

➢ Safety Equipment &amp;amp; Personal Protective Equipment (PPE) requirements

TDDC shall ensure that appropriate PPE compliant to national standards is supplied to TDDC’s personnel involved in the WORK. PEDEC intends to use water-based mud and hence TDDC’s Personnel that may be exposed to this must be provided with appropriate PPE.

TDDC shall be solely liable for all costs in equipping its own and all other personnel on contract to, or under the control of, TDDC with the required safety equipment. TDDC will provide to all TDDC’s Personnel appropriate PPE throughout the WORK. Any defective PPE will be replaced without delay. Personnel without the correct PPE will not be allowed to continue work, until replacement PPE has been issued. If any person is disallowed to work owing to inadequate PPE, then PEDEC will deduct his day- rate from TDDC’s invoice.

TDDC shall detail in the QHSE plan the PPE proposed for issue to the crew. This proposal should include the arrangements for replacement (due to wear and tear and damage) of this equipment.

➢ This well is committed to develop for oil in a responsible manner that promotes employees'

and local inhabitants' health.

➢ A trained Paramedic and proper MEDIVAC equipment and ambulance shall be stationed on

the drilling rig during the drilling campaign.

➢ An emergency medical evacuation plan shall be coordinated, and shall keep in touch with

emergency response centres manned by qualified doctor 24-hours per day in Tehran or Ahwaz

city.

➢ Medical emergency report and investigation shall be performed as soon as possible in

accordance with Medical Emergency Procedures.

➢ TDDC shall, as a minimum, conduct the WORK and provide personnel, equipment, facilities

and services consistent with good oil field practices and OGP Guidelines. An assessment of

the Contract Area with regard to “Environmental and Occupational Health Risks” and “Local

Medical Support” shall be provided by TDDC in the QHSE plan. This assessment and the

resulting “Health Risk Assessment Score” will be discussed with PEDEC, and a final

assessment will be agreed.

➢ To ensure the fitness of those personnel engaged to undertake the WORK, TDDC shall

conduct periodic medical examinations of its staff and ensure that its subcontractors’ personnel

are also subject to periodic medical examination. As a minimum this shall include pre-hire

medical examinations of temporary local personnel and bi-annual medical examinations of its

permanent and expatriate staff. Evidence should be made available to PEDEC to verify an

appropriate program of immunizations consistent with the recommendations of the World

Health Organization for the area of operations.

➢ TDDC shall be responsible for the cost of medical examinations as required to provide PEDEC

with medical certificates which confirm the fitness of TDDC’s assigned personnel for the

WORK. Each drilling rig shall be equipped with a hospital, doctor and an ambulance type-2

level.

➢ Drug Abuse and Control

➢ TDDC warrants that it shall obtain valid written consent of all Personnel to a search of property

of such Personnel when PEDEC suspects that such Personnel have in their possession drugs

and/or other prohibited substances or any other item likely to prejudice safety or security.

PEDEC will provide a representative to attend the search.

➢ TDDC warrants that it shall obtain valid written consent of all Personnel to medical testing for

the presence of drugs or other prohibited substances.

14.2 H2S Monitoring and Prevention Measure

The purpose of this procedure is to inform all personnel of the hazardous nature of H2S, the safety concerns during Drilling and maintenance operations performed onshore in the Drilling sites and the precautionary measures, which are in place for the Drilling sites onshore facilities.

The Operations Assistant is responsible for:

– Organising training and certification.

– The implementation of this procedure, ensuring that all personnel are informed of the danger

associated with H2S, and that all training, instruction and supervision are in place so that

personnel receive sufficient information on the hazards of H2S and precautionary measures.

➢ 4.0 H2S PROCEDURE

➢ 4.1 Nature and Effects of Hydrogen Sulphide (H2S)

Hydrogen Sulphide (H2S) is a highly toxic gas, even in small doses. It is colourless and at low concentrations has a strong odour that is often described as that of rotten eggs. Low concentrations of H2S hinders the ability of an individual to think clearly by affecting the nervous system.

Soluble in both water (4 volume gas in 1 volume water at 0°C) and liquid hydrocarbons, H2S is flammable and will form an explosive mixture with a concentration between 4.3% and 46% by volume. Auto-ignition occurs at 500ºF (260ºC), which is a very low ignition point when compared to other gases present.

Gas Explosive Limit (Vol. % gas)

H2S 4.3% – 46%

Methane 5.3% - 14%

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 200

Ethane 3.3% - 12.5%

Propane 2.4% - 9.5%

H2S burns with a blue flame and produces Sulphur dioxide (SO2), less toxic than H2S but very irritating to eyes and lungs and may cause serious injury.

H2S in combination with water forms a weak acid that will attack metals in the same manner as Carbon Dioxide or Oxygen (corrosive to all electro-chemical series metals). H2S embrittles steel and other materials. This phenomenon is also known as stress corrosion, Sulphide embrittlement and Hydrogen embrittlement.

H2S is heavier than air with a specific gravity 1.189, boiling point: -60.3°C (-76.5°F), melting point: - 82.9°C (-117.2°F).

➢ 4.2 Presence of H2S

All hydrocarbon bearing reservoirs encountered on the Yaran contain H2S. There is therefore a major safety concern during production and maintenance operations performed in the Yaran.

H2S concentration in Yaran gas arriving from well is 0.07 %, equivalent to 1750 ppm (parts per million).

➢ 4.3 H2S Hazards and Symptoms

Extremely toxic (almost as toxic as Hydrogen Cyanide) and 5 to 6 times as toxic as Carbon Monoxide, H2S can be only smelt at low concentrations (1-50 ppm). Beyond 50 ppm, the sense of smell is lost after about 15 minute’s exposures.

Therefore, DO NOT DEPEND ON SMELL TO DETECT H2S.

H2S is irritating, asphyxiating and very poisonous. It irritates the eyes and throat at low concentrations (30-150 ppm). At 500 ppm it causes dizziness and unconsciousness within 20 minutes.

Concentrations of 1,000 ppm of H2S cause immediate unconsciousness and death quickly follows unless artificial respiration and/or oxygen treatment is promptly applied (see the following table of H2S toxicity). Death may occur even if the individual is removed to fresh air at once.

H2S poisoning is not cumulative like mercury, lead or radioactivity. Repeated short exposures will not have same effect as one lengthy exposure.

Table 1: H2S Toxicity

H2S concentrations(Ppm, in air)Effects Observed4-6 Easily detected, moderate odour10-20 Unpleasant odour. Possible eye irritation.Threshold Limit Value (TLV) fixed at 10 ppm (15mg/m³) for an eight-hour, five-day week.

➢ Precautions

Escape mask with H2S cartridges (Draeger Parat II type) will be provided for personnel entering the Gas Treatment Plant. Sufficient number of escape masks will be provided to allow personal issue for all workers on the facility. Escape masks should be used for evacuation only and not for search and rescue operation, nor for working in an H2S environment.

Prior to any work being carried out in confined spaces and/or where the presence of H2S is possible, work permits and entry permits shall be obtained as detailed in the following procedures:

Entry to Vessel and Confined Spaces

The Drilling Permit to Work System

13...1 Search and Rescue Equipment

For the emergency teams and to allow the safe intervention of the team mate (Buddy System) in an emergency, a sufficient number of sets of Self-Contained Breathing Apparatus (SCBA) shall be provided at readily accessible places in the Drilling sites. Breathing apparatus will be used at the source area or in any area that has more than 10 ppm of H2S in the air.

The use of SCBA should be strictly restricted to safety and rescue operations.

The SCBA will be of positive pressure type, 30’ autonomy (6l x 200 bars), full-face mask.

Refer to the following for more detailed instruction and guidance: Breathing Apparatus.

➢ Treatment

Inhaled H2S often acts so quickly on the respiratory system that there is not time to call for medical assistance before an attempt should be made to revive the victim. Remove the patient to fresh air as quickly as possible. If breathing has stopped, is weak and intermittent, or is present in only occasional gasps, start artificial respiration at once. Continue this without interruption until natural breathing is restored. Summon medical help and treat for shock.

Treatment of life threatening H2S poisoning, characterised by loss of consciousness and associated respiratory failure, is aimed at:

- Maintaining respiration by supportive measures. Oxygen resuscitator must be used as soon as

possible,

- Treatments of local irritant effects of H2S gas on the eyes and mucous membranes of respiratory

tract by supportive measures and medical treatment,

- Enhancing detoxification by administration of antidotes.

13...1 Maintaining Respiration

Immediately remove victim from the hazardous area to fresh air while wearing SCBA* and using the buddy system (i.e., 2 people, 1 rescuing, 1 in stand by in safe area).

Immediately call for Medical Services.

Note (*): The DRAEGER PARAT Escape mask is not considered as protective equipment for rescue/intervention. 30’ capacity SCBA’s must be worn to remove the victim from contaminated area.

First Aid to ensure a clear airway is essential to preserve life until the Doctor arrives to render medical treatment.

First aid actions.

Check mouth of victim (false teeth, chewing gum, etc.) and clear if needed. If he is breathing, maintain at rest and administer O2 if available.

If breathing has ceased or is laboured, start artificial respiration to clear lungs of contaminated air. Prior to applying mouth-to-mouth respiration, try to expel gas from victim’s lungs by pressing down the chest, to prevent rescuer himself from being gassed by breathed H2S. Apply O2 resuscitator as soon as available on site to support respiration, once the victim resumes breathing spontaneously.

If it is impossible to move victim to fresh air, apply resuscitator immediately after checking victim’s mouth as above.

The role of oxygen in the treatment of H2S poisoning is essential: this is the fastest method for counteracting the effects of H2S inhalation.

Keep the victim at rest and prevent the victim from becoming cold.

13...2 Enhancing Detoxification

Treatments to enhance detoxification exist but require extreme care and high medical knowledge. They will therefore only be carried out by a qualified medical practitioner.

13...3 First Aid and Medical Equipment

➢ Mustering and Evacuation

Escape routes will be provided from working areas to the muster points. Evacuation may be required once at the mustering points (Mustering and Evacuation Procedure) An accurate wind forecast must be available at all times. The wind direction shall be shown by VISIBLE WIND INDICATORS such as windsocks, illuminated at night.

Four designated Muster Points located at perimeter exit gates are provided, and are equipped with suitable means of communication.

➢ H2S Detection and Alarm

In the Drilling sites where H2S concentrations might be encountered in all gas and condensate processing areas, efficient means are provided and used for detecting H2S presence and measuring its concentration.

Local area Audible and Visual alarms are connected to the detector system, which also gives an alarm in the control room.

All personnel on the facility are equipped with Personal H2S Detectors fitted with an Audible &amp;amp; Visual Alarm.

The Gas Treatment Plant is provided with a dedicated general alarm system.

➢ H2S Training

H2S training and personal certification is required for everybody prior to working in a Drilling site.

All personnel working on the plant shall be instructed as to the hazard of H2S and the available personal safety equipment, H2S detectors, alarms, ventilation procedures, briefing areas and evacuation procedures.

Training will take place at newcomer arrival and will include the following:

- Induction and briefing of all new arrivals on the dangers of H2S and the correct procedures to

follow.

- The use of Personal H2S Detectors, and escape sets.

- The use and calibration of all gas monitoring equipment.

- Recognition of alarms and action to be taken.

- Muster at Safe Briefing Areas.

- The ‘Buddy System’.

- Emergency actions to assist recovery of H2S victims.

- Explanation of the contingency plan.

Information relating to safety measures in the event of a H2S Emergency will be posted around the installation.

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 204

14.3 Well Control

1) Installation Requirements of BOP Stack and Control System

➢ The distance between BOP control lines and relief lines shall be not less than 1m. Steel Gap Bridge should

be placed in such a way to protect lines from damage by vehicle crossing. Sundries shall not be packed

on the control line shield, on which welding operations are forbidden.

➢ The air supply shall be separated from that of driller’s console and directly connected to the main air

supply system, and shall be equipped with liquid/gas separators. Excessive bending and pinching on air

hoses are forbidden.

➢ Electric power to BOP control system shall be directly from the main switch of the power board, and

controlled by independent switches.

➢ Accumulators shall be in good condition, charged at its rated value, and ready for work at any time.

2) Installation Requirements of Choke Lines and Kill Lines, and Relief Lines

a) Beside the drilling spool, one manual and one HCR gate valves on the side of chock manifold and

two manual gate valves on the side of kill manifold shall be equipped, and the manual gate valve

close to the spool shall be always open.

b) The high-pressure co-flex hose shall be installed as straight as possible and fixed by anchor after

installation.

c) Relief lines (burning line and bleed line):

• No field welding is permitted on relief lines， and the connecting style of relief lines must be

flanged.

• The arrangement of relief lines shall take wind directions, dwellings, roads, oil tank areas, power

lines and other facilities into consideration.

• While the two lines go in the same trend, the distance between the lines shall be at least 0.3m,

and the two lines shall be fixed independently.

• The outlets of bleed line connecting with choke manifold shall be in a safe area which is at least

300m. The bleed line on the kill manifold side connecting with drilling spool shall be prepared and

connected when necessary (about 70m from the wellhead).

• At every 10m~15m, turning points, and outlets, the lines shall be supported and fixed by cement

foundations with anchor bolts or ground anchors. Hanging portions shall be supported firmly. If

crossing the stumbling blocks such as brooks or ponds is needed, metal bridging supports are

necessary.

• Pre-buried anchor bolts for cement foundations shall not be less than 20mm or 3/4" in diameter,

and greater than 0.5m in length.

3) Pressure test value and requirements

a) A pressure test shall be conducted successfully where there is no pressure decline in 5 minutes for

a low-pressure test and a minimum of 10 minutes for a high-pressure test.

b) Prior to each high-pressure test, a low-pressure test with 200-300 psi shall be carried out to verify

sealing capability at the circumstance of low pressure and avoid washing out sealing elements.

c) High pressure test should be to the rated working pressure of ram preventers or the rated working

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 205

pressure of the wellhead, whichever is the lowest, or to the rating required in DRILLING ROGRAM.

Variable bore rams should be initially pressure tested on the largest and smallest OD pipe sizes

that may be used during the OPERATION. Annular preventers, with a joint of drill pipe installed,

should be tested to 70% of their working pressure. The high test for Kelly cocks, kill lines and choke

lines should be to their rated working pressures, each part in the choke manifold is tested

respectively to its rated working pressures, the relief lines be pressure tested to no less than

1,450psi (10Mpa).

d) When casing cup tester is used, the above test pressure should not exceed 80% of the internal

yield pressure (burst pressure) of the exposed casing.

e) The control system of BOP shall be pressure tested to 3,000psi (21Mpa) for its reliability before

putting into use.

f) Except that the BOP control system is tested with hydraulic oil, other well control equipment shall

be pressure tested by clear water.

g) Testing tools

• All required sizes of the plug type testers, and cup type testers should be available for pressure

testing purpose.

• When the cup type tester(s) is used for pressure test, the connection of the cup testers has to be

checked, to ensure it will stand the load during the high pressures test. For surface casing, the cup

tester is to be set in middle of the first casing joint in the top, so the first casing connection (between

wellhead and casing head housing) below the well head is to be tested too while testing BOP and

wellhead.

4) Pressure test frequency

All ram type preventers, annular BOP, valves, choke and kill lines, choke manifold and kelly valves, and safety valves should be pressure tested at the following frequencies:

• Prior to installation where possible.

• After installation of wellhead and BOP assembly and prior to drilling.

• After the disconnection or repair of any pressure containment seal in the BOP stack, choke line,

or choke manifold, but limited to the affected component.

• Prior to drilling into a confirmed or suspected high-pressure zone.

• At any time requested by the COMPANY REPRESENTATIVE.

• In any case, every one month or during the first trip after the one-month interval with a maximum

interval of 45 days. This frequency is not required for shear rams. As a minimum, the shear rams

are to be tested prior to resume DRILLING OPERATION after 9 5/8” casing string has been set

and adequate WOC.

5) WORK Requirement of Well Control Equipment

• With shut-in casing pressure lower than 1,000psi, while snubbing with annular preventer, drill tools

shall have 18-deg shoulder tool joints, and the snubbing velocity shall not be greater than 0.2m/s.

• After shut off the annular preventer or ram preventer, with the shut-in casing pressure not greater

than 2,000psi, drill tools are allowed to pull up and down with the velocity less than 0.2m/s,

however, rotating or tool joint passing through are forbidden.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 206

• While drilling through pay zone and thereafter, BOP function (close-open) test for the BOPs shall

be performed regularly.

• Kill manifolds shall not be used for drilling fluid fill-in.

• The maximum allowed shut-in casing pressure shall be remarkably indicated on choke manifolds.

• All gate valves of well control manifolds shall be tagged with serial numbers, and be indicated with

on/Off.

• In case shear rams were used, after shearing the drill pipe with shear rams, retrieve the upper

sheared drill pipe, lower a drill pipe joint with TIW valve installed into the hole and close pipe rams

on it to prevent the possibility of shear ram’s failure.

6) Management of Well Control Equipment

• Well control equipment used by the drilling crew shall be managed and operated by designated

personnel with clear post responsibilities.

• Well site shall have specified component storeroom and rubber element storeroom with air

conditioners respectively. The temperature of storerooms shall meet storage requirements of

components and rubber elements.

• At least 1 set is available as back-up for every type ram assembly in the well site. Ram preventer

side door seals and ram rubber, every type gasket and bolt etc. should be enough for backup.

7) Killing Method and Shut-in Method

• Driller's method is recommended for well killing.

• The hard shut-in method is recommended for shutting in the well.

8) Well Control During Drilling

a) Shut in Procedure in While on Bottom

• Position kelly (if in use) and tool joint so that tool joints are clear of sealing elements.

• Shut off pumps, check for flow.

• If flow is noted, close the annular preventer and open remote controlled choke line valve

(HCR).

• If the well has been closed using the annular preventer, check space out (pipe rams vs. toll

joint relative position) and close pipe rams and ram locks

• Bleed off pressure between pipe rams and annular preventer (if possible).

• Record shut-in drillpipe and casing pressures.

• Record volume gain and mark pits.

• Weigh mud in suction pits.

• Check blowout preventer and manifold for leaks.

• Check flowline and choke exhaust for flow.

• Check accumulator pressure.

b) Shut in Procedure in While Tripping

• If a flow is detected, install full opening safety valve and close same.

• Position tool joints clear of the sealing elements and close annular preventer and open remote

controlled choke line valve (HCR).

• Make up grey valve (dart type inside BOP on full open safety valve). Then Make up kelly or

top drive (insert a pup joint or single between safety valve and top drive) and open safety valve.

• Record casing pressure.

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 207

• Check blowout preventer, stack, and manifold for leaks.

• Check valves for correct position.

• Check accumulator pressure.

• Begin stripping to bottom.

c) Shut in Procedure for Empty Wells

• Try to RIH with drill pipe as more as possible if hole condition permits, and the drill pipe must

be installed a kelly cock or safety valve.

• Close the annular preventer and open remote controlled choke line valve (HCR).

• If the well has been closed using the annular preventer, check space out (pipe rams vs. tool

joint relative position) and close pipe rams and ram locks

• Bleed off pressure between pipe rams and annular preventer (jf possible).

• Record casing pressure

9) Well Control During Running Casing

The well control while running casing is important and critical, best practices shall be followed, including but not limited to:

• Change out pipe rams to casing rams prior to running casing into hydrocarbon bearing formation,

if possible.

• The crossover between casing and drill pipe has to be available, and good practice is to make it

up with a joint of DP and keep on V-door.

10) Shut in Procedure During Logging.

Cable shall be pull out of the hole immediately when overflow is found during logging. If pit gain exceeds the specified amount, the cable shall be cut immediately and treat the overflow in accordance with the shut-in procedure for empty wells. Continuing to pull the cable through the closed annular preventer is not recommended.

11) Well Control During Testing

• No difference to while DRILLING OPERATION, since either there is testing string in the well (same

as Shut-in procedure while on bottom), or tripping the string. (Same as shut-in procedure while

tripping).

• Likely there is some crude in the testing string, and precaution has to be taken while circulating

the crude out of hole during killing operations to ensure it is properly handled without causing

environmental impact.

• To minimize the formation damage, the killing fluids used shall be weighted brine if possible,

however in well control case when it occurs, killing well safely will take priority rather than protecting

formation from damage on selection of killing fluids.

12) Well Control During Stimulation

• Similar to during testing.

• For any residual or alive acid to be circulated out during killing operations, measures have to be

taken to treat the returned acid properly to avoid personnel from getting hurt, and without causing

any environmental damage.

13) Well Control Drills

South Azadegan Oil Field EPDS Project

Drilling &amp;amp; Completion of 15 Wells and Completion of 2 Work Over Wells

Contract No. Co. Prj. Dis. Dep. Doc. Rev. Well No.

CCA/C/1400/1 TDDC SAZP PM ENG WDP 02 195(F10)

TADBIR DRILLING DEVELOPMENT COMPANY P | 208

Drills shall be conducted only when hole conditions and other rig activities permit. Drills should be scheduled so that crews can practice drills while on bottom, while tripping in and while out of the hole. Each operating shift shall perform at least one BOP drill under different situations per month.

14) Basic Requirement of Well Control

• For 13-5/8”10 K BOP, BOP should be schemed as follows: upper rams 5”, middle rams are shear

rams, lower rams is variable rams (2-7/8” to 5”). For easily connecting, armored fire-resistant

10000psi and ID 4-1/16" flexible hoses are recommended as kill/choke lines which connected with

BOP directly.

• The BOP stack and choke manifold, kill line and choke line, HCR and manual gate valves, inside

BOP (including kelly cock) have to be H2S trimmed and stand the working temperature from -10

degrees C (in case of doing N2 for DST) to the maximum up to 100 deg C (estimated highest

temperature condition).

• The BOP equipment shall be visually inspected, function tested, and hydrostatically pressure

tested during commissioning and Acceptance of Drilling Unit prior to Commencement Date, then

upon installation on a wellhead, prior to drilling any hole section with a potential hydrocarbon

bearing reservoir, after breaking any BOP connection (such as after installation of an additional

wellhead section after running casing), and at least every 14-21 days while the BOP is installed on

the Well.

• CONTRACTOR shall submit the current well control procedures manual of CONTRACTOR to

COMPANY for review and approval by COMPANY. Valid IWCF Well Control Certificates (or IADC

equivalent) must be held by all tool pushers, Drillers (optional) at Drilling Supervisor level and

Drillers and Assistant Drillers at Driller level, as further specified in CONTRACTOR Personnel HSE

Requirements.

• The accumulator must be of sufficient capacity so that it is possible to close one (1) ram and the

bag, open a ram and the bag, re-close a ram and the bag and retain 1200psi accumulator pressure.

• The “OPEN” and “CLOSED” operating positions of BOP activators of the rams, annular, and

remote actuated valves must be clearly identified on the BOP master control panel and remote

panels, in EnglIIF in addition to the native language of the majority of the senior drilling crew.

• Shear-ram BOP activator must have a flap-cover to ensure only pre-meditated and deliberated

activation and to prevent accidental WORK.

• All lines and connections in the choke and kill manifold, standpipe manifold, choke and kill lines

and high-pressure mud lines should be welded hard-piping with weld-on FMC Weco “hammer-lug”

1502 unions (or Western equivalent, such as Anson) or weld-on API flanges. The well control

system should not include any threaded screw-on hammer-lug unions, flanges, or other threaded

screw-on connections.

• All adapter or spacer spools between the wellhead and BOP must be manufactured in accordance

with API 6A, and must be complete with all required studs, nuts, and ring gasket seals for both

lower and upper connections; minimum spare parts should include two (2) OEM spare studs

complete with nuts and two spare OEM ring gasket seals for both bottom and top connections.

• There should be a wide-bore gas vent line from the “poor-boy” degasser; the line and line outlet

must be securely anchored, the line outlet must be a safe distance from the well cellar, rig floor,

choke manifold, and mud logging unit.

• Well Control Equipment Welding: All welds in the well control system must be NDT inspected and

records maintained of all welding and weld inspections, including choke and kill manifold,

standpipe manifold, mud pump discharge manifold, choke and kill lines.

• Well Control Rig Floor Information: The following must be posted on the rig floor (in the Driller’s

shack / “dog-house”): Well Shut-In Procedure, Well Information Sheet (showing casing and hole

sizes, depths, and such like), Kill Sheet (including most recent slow circulation rate pressures,

formation integrity leak-off test information and MAASP, present mud weight, present drill string

and well volumes, and such like).

• The derrick substructure clear height (rotary table to ground level) needs to be enough for fitting in

above BOP configuration (including a tubing head and a bell nipple). This need to be double

checked in rig tender process. According to the calculation of height of BOP stack, the substructure

clear height is at least 9 m.

• In the case if drilling 6-1/8" hole becomes necessary, or using 3-1/2" drill pipe for the cleanup run

(including drill 7" shoe track), then taper drilling string (5" DP + 3-1/2" DP) is to be run, so a pair

variable ram be installed in the single ram preventer. The defect installing variable ram in single

ram preventer is that the ram may be damaged and the string fall in hole bottom due to the ram

hanging up too much weight when the drill pipe be cut. If the pipe ram be installed in the single

ram preventer and the variable ram be installed in the double ram preventer, it would be very

dangerous due to the variable ram failure in frequently using.

1. General Information

1.1 Introduction

The Sepehr-Jufair Field situated in the Abadan Plain, Southwest of Khuzestan Province, about 40 km West of Ahwaz. The Sepehr-Jufair field covers about 352 square kilometers area with approximately 23 kilometers long and 27 kilometers wide. Figure 1 illustrates the geographical situation of the Sepehr and Jufair gentle culminations that are located in the vicinity of South Azadegan and Yadavaran giant fields.

Figure 1: Geographical location of the Sepehr-Jufair field.

Figure 2: Location of Sepehr-Jufair Oil field – Side view of Structure

Sepehr-Jufair Field Development and

Production Operations Project

Well Drilling Program – SPH-03

Doc. No: SJD1-DDRGDP-DRDR-PLDR-3001-D03 Page 15 of 222

1.2 Well Information
